#### Tests (`/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/data`)

- No test removes the **last** row of a `VarDouble/VarInt/VarNominal/VarLong` (`VarDoubleTest.java:241` removes the middle one) — the `removeRow` no-op bug above is untested. `VarInstantTest` has no `removeRow` test at all. [test]
- No test for `IntervalMapping` (only `mapping/ArrayMappingTest.java`); `Mapping.range(...)` is exercised indirectly 20 times but never after `add/remove/shuffle`, so the `elements()` bug is invisible. [test]
- No tests for `RowComparators` (comparator contract, missing ordering), `TextTableUtil`, `FinBar`/`FinBarSize`, individual `GroupFun*` classes (all-missing groups, label min/max), `VarRange` error paths (typo names, malformed `a~`). [test]
- No cross-type missing-value contract test (table above): `getInt/getLong/getDouble` on a missing row for every Var type, `setDouble(NaN)` on INT/LONG/NOMINAL/INSTANT. [test]
- No `deepEquals` tests for `VarInstant` with missing rows, for `VarFloat` tolerance, or `VarString.deepEquals(var, tol)`. [test]
- No serialization round-trip tests for `VarNominal` with a lambda parser, `VSpots`, `VarLong/VarInstant` (default serialization). [test]
- No test that `MappedFrame.rvar(i).removeRow(...)` leaves the frame unchanged, nor that `mapVars` hides name-based access to dropped columns. [test]
- `SolidFrame.byVars` with different-length vars and with duplicate names are not tested. [test]
- `AbstractFrame.toSummary` is not tested on a machine with `availableProcessors() == 1` (cannot be, but the code path should not depend on CPU count). [test]

#### Summary (top 5)

1. **`removeRow` is a no-op for the last row** in `VarDouble`, `VarInt`, `VarNominal`, `VarLong`, and never shrinks `VarInstant` at all (`VarDouble.java:431`, `VarInstant.java:145`). Silent data corruption on a basic mutator; no test covers it.
2. **Missing-value contract is inconsistent across Var types** (table in section 2): `VarDouble.getInt(NaN)=0`, `VarLong.getDouble(missing)=9.2e18`, `VarNominal.setDouble(NaN)` assigns level 0, `VarInstant.getDouble(missing)` NPEs, `VarString.empty(n)` rows are not missing. Every generic algorithm that goes through `getDouble/isMissing` inherits these.
3. **Shared mutable `Mapping`**: `MappedFrame.rvar()` hands the frame's mapping to each `MappedVar`, whose `removeRow/clearRows` mutate it; `IntervalMapping.elements()` ignores its mutated list; `BoundVar.from(counts, vars)` never checks counts against sizes. Row indirection, the core of the frame model, can silently desynchronise.
4. **Type switches with missing cases**: `AbstractVar.copy()` (no FLOAT -> throws for mapped/bound floats), `Var.deepEquals`/`refComparator`/`VSpot.compareTo` (FLOAT/INSTANT compared as strings), `VarType.isNumeric` (excludes FLOAT/LONG), `Unique.of` (no LONG/FLOAT/INSTANT), `RowComparators.longComparator` never returns 0.
5. **Design debt**: the `Var` interface forces every type to implement ~20 accessors it does not support with four different exception types; unsupported/invalid input is often coerced silently (`VarBinary.fill(rows, 7)`, `VarRange` typos dropped, `SolidFrame.byVars` truncating to the shortest var, in-place vs copying `Transform.apply`). Pick one failure mode (throw `OperationNotAvailableException`/`IllegalArgumentException`) and one mutation policy, and document them on the interfaces.

### 2.3 ml.model base classes, tree, ensemble, boost, rule, bayes, knn, simple, meta

# Review: rapaio ml/model base classes, trees, ensembles, boosting, rules, bayes, knn, simple, meta

Root: `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib`. Analysis only, no code changed.

#### ml/model base classes (ClassifierModel, RegressionModel, ClassifierResult, RegressionResult, RunInfo)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:143` `capabilities()` returns `null` by default but `prepareFit` (line 281) unconditionally calls `capabilities().checkAtLearnPhase(...)`; any subclass that forgets the override NPEs at fit. `RegressionModel.capabilities()` is abstract. [inconsistency] Fix: make it abstract in ClassifierModel too.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:407` vs `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/RegressionModel.java:344` `getRandom()`: classifier uses `seed.hasDefaultValue()`, regression uses `seed.get() == 0`. Same intent, two idioms; a classifier explicitly `seed.set(0L)` still gets `hasDefaultValue()==true` so behaviour happens to match, but it is fragile. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:73` `poolSize` default `0` vs `RegressionModel.java:71` default `-1` (auto = cores-1). Same parameter, different default semantics between classifiers and regressors. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/RegressionModel.java:89` stopping hook param is named `"stopHook"` while the field and the classifier version are `stoppingHook`. Shows up in `fullName()`/`equalOnParams`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/RegressionModel.java:59` `public final Function<H,Boolean> DEFAULT_STOPPING_HOOK` is a non-static, non-transient instance field holding a non-serializable lambda in a `Serializable` class; also `runningHook`/`stoppingHook` use `java.util.function.Consumer/Function` (not `SConsumer/SFunction` as ClassifierModel does). Serializing any fitted regression model (RTree, RForest, GBTRegressionModel...) with default hooks will throw `NotSerializableException`. [bug] Fix: use `SConsumer`/`SFunction`, make the constant `static`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:242` `hasLearned()` / field `learned` vs `RegressionModel.java:225` `isFitted()` / field `hasLearned`. Same concept, three names. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:280-295` classifier checks capabilities *before* parsing target names, regression (`RegressionModel.java:277-292`) parses first then checks; regression builds the input complement by hand instead of `VarRange.parseComplementVarNames`. Duplicated logic that has already drifted. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:372-384` and `RegressionModel.java:304-320` `FitSetup.targetVars` is filled but never read anywhere; `PredSetup` mutable public fields for an immutable value. [design] Fix: records.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:253` default weights unnamed, `RegressionModel.java:237` named `"weights"`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:305-320` `predict` is `final`, in `RegressionModel.java:261-275` it is not. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:56` self-typed generics `M extends ClassifierModel<M,R,H>` with `H extends RunInfo<M>` force `@SuppressWarnings("unchecked")` casts `(M) this` in every param field; `newInstance()` returns `ClassifierModel<M,R,H>` rather than `M`, so callers of `newInstance()` on the abstract type lose the concrete type and must cast (see CForest/RForest/BaggingMode). [design] Fix: `public abstract M newInstance();`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierResult.java:238` inside the loop over `targetName` the confusion matrix is built with `df.rvar(model.firstTargetName())` instead of `df.rvar(targetName)`. Wrong for multi-target results. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierResult.java:86-90` `switch` on target type without `default`: a target type other than NOMINAL/BINARY/INT silently produces no class var, then `firstClasses()` returns null. [design] Fix: throw.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierResult.java:62-72` `copy(...)` shares the `Var`/`Frame` instances of `from` (shallow), while the name suggests a copy. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierResult.java:159,189` returns the internal `LinkedHashMap` directly; callers can mutate the result. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/RegressionResult.java:274,306` `rsquare = 1 - rss/tss`: when `tss == 0` (constant target) this is NaN or -Inf; no guard. `toSummary` also recomputes R^2 instead of reading `rsquare`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/RegressionResult.java:253` `buildComplete()` is public and mutable state: `residuals`/`tss` are NaN until a model remembers to call it; every regressor duplicates the call. [design] Fix: compute lazily or in the base `predict` after `corePredict`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/RegressionResult.java:292` `skip(10)` on the frame summary text couples the result summary to the exact line layout of `SolidFrame.toSummary`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/RunInfo.java:24` plain class with only final fields and accessors; the three static factories differ only by bound and are not used to add behaviour. [nit] Fix: `record RunInfo<M>(M model, int run)`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:83` `runs` default 100 and `rowSampler`/`poolSize` live in the base class although most models (CTree, NaiveBayes, OneRule, ZeroRule, kNN) ignore them; they still print in `fullName()` when non-default and confuse `equalOnParams`. [design]

#### tree (CTree, RTree, RowPredicate)

##### CTree

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:345` `predictPoint` missing-value fallthrough: `for (int i = 1; i < dict.size(); i++)` normalizes by `w` starting at index 1. `dv` was built with `emptyByLabels(false, dict)` so index 0 is the first real class, which is never divided by `w`. Its density is inflated by the summed child weight and `findBestLabel()` is biased toward the first class whenever a predict row has a missing value on a split variable. [bug] Fix: start at `i = 0` (or `dv.normalize()`).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:259` depth stop is `node.depth > maxDepth`. Root has depth 0, so a node at depth == maxDepth still splits; `newDecisionStump()` (`maxDepth=1`) produces trees with two levels of splits. Same off-by-one in `RTree.java` `learnNode`. [bug] Fix: `node.depth >= maxDepth`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:260` `df.rowCount() <= minCount` stops a node with exactly `minCount` rows, while the Search implementations require children of size `>= minCount`; a node with `minCount+1` rows can never split anyway (children would be `< minCount`). Inconsistent definition of `minCount` (node vs child). [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:255-257` `df.rowCount()==0` reads `node.parent.bestLabel`; NPE if root is fitted on an empty frame (capabilities probably block it, but no explicit check). [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:264-284` `m = nodeVarSelector.mCount()` decremented only when a candidate is non-null, but `nextVarNames` already returns `m` names, so at most `m` candidates are ever tried; the `m <= 0` guard is dead unless `nextVarNames` returns more than `m`. Also `VarSelector.withVarNames(inputNames())` is computed once per fit, fine, but `nextVarNames(random)` for `VarSelector.all()` is called at every node. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:217` `ConcurrentLinkedQueue` in a strictly single-threaded loop. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:244` default pruning frame is the training frame; ReducedError pruning on training data can only ever collapse pure duplicates and is misleading; document or require `pruningDf`. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:140-146` field `searchMap` but param name `"testMap"`; line 175 field `pruning` but param name `"prunning"` (typo). These strings appear in `fullName()`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:180` `pruningDf` is a `Frame` parameter: it is copied into every `newInstance()` (all CForest trees), serialized with the model and printed by `fullName()`. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:298-304` `CTree.prune(df)` defaults `all=false` but `Pruning.prune(tree, df)` (`Pruning.java:187`) defaults `all=true`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:351-359` `additionalValidation` iterates all frame vars (including target and unused columns) and the error text says "can't predict" during fit. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:325` `predictPoint(CTree tree, ...)` takes the tree as parameter although it is an instance method and `this == tree`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:362-372` and `:395-406` node counting duplicated between `countNodes` and `toSummary`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:460` `equalOnParams` compares `fullName()` strings; a `Frame`-typed `pruningDf` or a `VarSelector` with an unstable `toString` breaks equality. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:99-108` `newCART` uses `Splitter.Random` (a randomized missing-value policy) as the default CART, while `newC45` uses `Weighted`; CART canonically uses surrogate splits. Documented? [design]

##### ctree.Search / Purity / Splitter / Pruning / Node / Candidate

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:152` `Binary.computeCandidate` builds `DensityTable.fromLabels(false, test, target, null)`: weights are ignored for the purity of binary variables while every other search uses them. Boosting (AdaBoost) with binary inputs silently trains unweighted stumps. [bug] Fix: pass `w`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:63,66` `int missingWeights` accumulates `double` weights, truncating them; `missingPenalty` is then wrong for fractional weights. [bug] Fix: `double`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:56-58` `NumericRandom` loops `do { split = random.nextInt(n) } while (isMissing)`: infinite loop when the test column is all-missing in that node. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:62,92` `DensityTable.empty(true, NUMERIC_DEFAULT_LABELS, levels)` creates rows `["?", "less-equals", "greater"]` and the code increments rows 0 and 1, so the "<=" group lands in the "?" row and the ">" group in "less-equals"; the "greater" row is always empty. Scores are unaffected (an empty row contributes nothing), but any purity that penalizes number of groups (GainRatio intrinsic info counts a 3rd empty group as 0, fine) or debugging output is misleading. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:120` `NumericBinary` split admissibility: `i >= minCount && i < len - minCount`. At index `i` the left group has `i+1` rows, so left needs `>= minCount+1` while right needs `>= minCount`. Asymmetric off-by-one. [bug] Fix: `i + 1 >= minCount && len - i - 1 >= minCount`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:222-223` `NominalBinary`, binary-target branch: levels are sorted by raw weight of class 0 (`dtWeights.get(k,0)`) instead of the *proportion* of class 0 within the level. The Breiman ordering trick only yields the optimal subset when sorting by proportion; sorting by absolute weight gives suboptimal splits. [bug] Fix: compare `get(k,0)/rowTotal(k)`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:268-271` `NominalBinary`, multi-class branch: checks `rowCounts[i] >= minCount` for the tested level but never checks that the complement ("other") has `>= minCount` rows. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:176-190,191-297` `missingPenalty` is applied in `NumericRandom`, `NumericBinary`, `Binary` but not in `NominalFull` / `NominalBinary`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:153,181,197` all use `DensityTable.hasColsWithMinimumCount(minCount, 2)`; that method (`/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/core/tools/DensityTable.java:288-300`) iterates rows and sums columns from `j = 1`, i.e. it *skips the first target class*. With `withMissing=false` column 0 is a real class, so a test level whose rows are all of the first class counts as 0 and the split may be wrongly rejected (or a split with a too-small group accepted). Name is also inverted (it counts rows). [bug] Fix in DensityTable: sum from `j = 0`, rename `hasRowsWithMinimumWeight`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:103,114` first pass uses `dt.colIndex().getIndex(df, targetName, i)`, second pass uses `df.getInt(rows[i], targetIndex)`; both are the same index but by two different routes. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:106` `values` is materialized for the whole column on every candidate evaluation at every node (`stream().mapToDouble().toArray()`); `df.getDouble(row, testIndex)` would avoid the allocation. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Splitter.java:91-92` `Majority`: `Collections.shuffle(lens)` uses the global unseeded RNG and `lens` is never read afterwards. Dead code that also makes the "seeded" tree non-reproducible in principle (no effect on output today). [bug/dead] Fix: delete the two lines; use `random` if tie-breaking was intended.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Splitter.java:144-146` `Weighted`: `p[i] /= psum` with `psum == 0` (all rows missing on the split var) yields NaN weights. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Splitter.java:157-158` `Weighted` returns fresh `VarDouble` weights not aligned by name with the original weights var; harmless but inconsistent with the other splitters which return `weights.mapRows`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Pruning.java:83,96` `ReducedError`: `maxAcc = -1` and the selection test is `delta >= maxAcc`, so a node whose pruning *decreases* accuracy (`-1 <= delta < 0`) is still selected and cut when it is the first/only candidate in that pass, even with `all == false`. The `pruned.add(id)` at line 102 only removes it from later passes. [bug] Fix: initialize `maxAcc = 0` (or require `delta >= 0` before choosing).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Pruning.java:95` `topDown.sum()` or `bottomUp.sum()` is 0 for nodes no pruning row reaches, giving NaN deltas that are silently skipped; leaves always have `delta == 0` and are "pruned" (no-op `cut()`) one per outer iteration, so the outer loop runs O(#nodes) full scans. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Pruning.java:116-118` `runningHook` is invoked with `RunInfo.forClassifier(tree, nodes.size())`, abusing `run` to mean node count. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Pruning.java:169` `bottomUpCollect` returns zeros for a row that matches no child predicate (missing value on split var) so those rows are dropped from the pruning estimate, while prediction uses the weighted-children fallback. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Node.java:40-51` all state public and mutable (`leaf`, `density`, `bestCandidate`...), `Candidate.java:34-45` is a `record` with a mutable `List` and an `addGroup` mutator. Records should be immutable. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Purity.java:48-79` `GiniGain` hand-rolled while InfoGain/GainRatio delegate to DensityTable; `ChiSquare` returns `1 - pValue` which is not a gain and is not comparable to `minGain` semantics. [inconsistency]

##### RTree

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:241-247` candidate search uses `.parallel()` on the common ForkJoin pool while sharing one `java.util.Random random` between threads. `Random` is thread-safe but the order in which threads draw numbers is not deterministic, so `seed` does not give reproducible trees (Search.NumericBinary draws `random.nextDouble()` for tie-breaks, `Splitter.Random` draws in the caller thread afterwards but the RNG state already diverged). It also ignores `poolSize` and nests inside RForest's own parallel fit. [bug] Fix: sequential search, or one `SplittableRandom`/`Random` per candidate derived from the node seed.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:234` when `node.weight == 0`, `node.weight = node.parent.value` copies the parent's *value* into the child's *weight*. Should be `node.parent.weight` (or 0). The weight feeds the missing-value fallback in `predict` (line 301). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:192` root `Node` is created with `depth = 1`, while `CTree` root has `depth = 0`, and the stop test is `depth >= maxDepth` here vs `depth > maxDepth` in CTree. Result: `RTree.maxDepth=2` and `CTree.maxDepth=1` both mean "one split" (`newDecisionStump` in each class uses a different number). [inconsistency] Fix: same convention (root depth 0, stop at `>=`) in both.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:237` `maxDepth.get() == -1 ? Integer.MAX_VALUE : ...` is dead: the validator at line 123 rejects `x <= 0`, and CTree uses `-1` for "unlimited". [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:187` `capabilities().checkAtLearnPhase(...)` is re-run in `coreFit` although `prepareFit` already did it. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:191` `this.varSelector.get().withVarNames(inputNames());` result discarded; the same call is repeated at 194. Dead code. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:192` root predicate is an inline lambda `(row, frame) -> true` while CTree uses `RowPredicate.all()`; the lambda's `toString` is meaningless in summaries. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:243` `test.get(df.type(testCol))` returns `null` for a type not in the map and NPEs inside a parallel stream (wrapped, no variable name); CTree throws a descriptive `IllegalArgumentException`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:301` missing-value fallback returns `Mean.of(weights)` as the aggregated weight while CTree sums child weights; also allocates two `VarDouble` per row. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:364-366` `boostUpdate` calls `getRandom()` on each call, so with a fixed seed every boosting round restarts the identical random sequence for `Splitter.Random` routing of missing rows. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:128` `maxSize` parameter is declared, validated and printed but never read. Dead parameter. [dead]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:153` param field `test` (name `"testMap"`) vs CTree field `searchMap` (also `"testMap"`); `minScore` (RTree) vs `minGain` (CTree) for the same threshold; `loss` present only in RTree. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:66` `RTree extends GBTRtree` (a boosting abstraction living in `ml/model/boost`) so the tree package depends on the boost package that depends on it. [design] Fix: make `boostUpdate` an interface in `tree` or move `GBTRtree` there.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Node.java:67-87` `boostUpdate` recomputes leaf values with `additiveScalarMinimizer(y, fx)` and routes rows with `VarDouble.fill(1)` weights: sample weights passed to `GBTRegressionModel.fit` are ignored for the leaf values and for the `Majority` splitter. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Search.java:68-78` `NumericBinary`: `rows = rowsComplete()`; if the test column is all-missing in the node `rows.length == 0` and `rows[0]` throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`. [bug] Fix: return `Optional.empty()` when `rows.length < 2*minCount`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Search.java:105` loop bounds `i in [minCount, len - minCount - 2]`: left group has `i+1 >= minCount+1` rows and right has `len-i-1 >= minCount+1`, so both sides need strictly more than `minCount`; with `minCount=1` a single row can never be isolated. Differs from the ctree version (asymmetric) and from the `NominalBinary` check (`>= minCount`). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Search.java:164-172` `NominalFull` requires *every* level of the variable (including levels with zero rows in this node) to have `>= minCount` rows, otherwise no candidate. After any split, subtrees rarely contain every level, so nominal variables become unusable below the root for `newC45()` (which maps NOMINAL to `NominalFull`). CTree's `NominalFull` only needs 2 levels with `>= minCount`. [bug] Fix: require at least two valid groups and drop empty groups from the candidate.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Search.java:242-247` `NominalBinary` recomputes `wosRemain` from scratch for each level: O(levels^2); `WeightedOnlineStat` supports subtraction-free combination so `wos` minus `onlineStats[i]` could be precomputed once. Also ties go to the first level, `random` unused. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Search.java:125` `df.getDouble(rows[i], testName)` looks up by name while the rest of the method uses `testIndex`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Splitter.java:71-107` `Majority` picks the group by *weight* and breaks ties with `random`; ctree `Splitter.Majority` picks by *count* and has a dead global `shuffle`. Same name, different semantics. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Splitter.java:149` `createMapList(int capacity, ...)` ignores `capacity`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Candidate.java` is a mutable class with `getScore()/getTestName()` while `ctree/Candidate` is a record with `score()/testName()`; `addGroup` here throws on duplicate predicates, the ctree one does not. [inconsistency] Fix: one shared `Candidate` type in `tree`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/SearchPayload.java:29` public mutable fields, no `Serializable`, no constructor validation (`splits` vs array sizes fixed but `totalVar` can be left 0). [design]
- Duplication: `RTree.coreFit`/`learnNode`/`predict`/`buildSummary` and `CTree.coreFit`/`learnNode`/`predictPoint`/`buildSummary` are near-copies (queue-based growth, `QueueNode` record, missing-value fallback, tree printing). Two `Node`, two `Candidate`, two `Splitter`, two `Search` enums with the same shape. [design] Fix: a generic `TreeGrower<N,C>` and a shared `Splitter` (the ctree `Splitter.Weighted` variant is the only one without a regression counterpart).

##### rowpredicate

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rowpredicate/NominalNotEqual.java:37-40` and `NominalNotInSet.java:35-38`: for a *missing* nominal value the predicate returns `true` (`!"x".equals("?")`). In a `nomEqual/nomNotEqual` (or `nomInSet/nomNotInSet`) pair the missing rows therefore always match the second predicate, so `Splitter.Weighted/Random/Majority` and the `predictPoint` fallback never see them as missing. Binary and numeric predicates return `false` for missing on both sides. Missing-value policy is silently bypassed for nominal binary splits (the default for NOMINAL in `CTree`, `newCART`, `RTree.newCART`). [bug] Fix: return `false` when missing (unless `"?"` is explicitly in the set).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rowpredicate/NumLessEqual.java:37` relies on `NaN <= x == false` for missing, while `NumGreater.java:37` checks `isMissing` explicitly; `NumLess`/`NumGreaterEqual` mixed. Works for DOUBLE, but an INT var with missing returns `Integer.MIN_VALUE`-ish or throws depending on `getDouble` semantics. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rowpredicate/NominalInSet.java:31` and `NominalNotInSet.java` lack `serialVersionUID` while all sibling records declare one. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/rowpredicate/*` every `test(row, df)` resolves `testName` to a column by string at each row; trees on wide frames pay a hash lookup per node per row. [design] Fix: resolve once per predict call (`df.varIndex`) or cache index per frame identity.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/tree/RowPredicate.java:43` `@FunctionalInterface` on a `Serializable` interface allows unserializable lambdas (see `RTree.java:192`), defeating `Serializable` on the tree. [design]

#### boost (AdaBoost, GBTRegressionModel, GBTClassifierModel, GBTRtree)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:117,149` `k = firstTargetLevels().size() - 1` then `alpha = shrinkage * (log((1-err)/err) + log(k - 1.0))`. `levels()` does not contain `"?"` (see `VarNominal.levels()`), so for a binary target `k = 1` and `log(k-1) = log(0) = -Infinity`: every alpha is `-Infinity`, misclassified weights are multiplied by `exp(-inf) = 0`, and prediction densities become `-inf/NaN`. For `K` classes the SAMME term should be `log(K-1)`, the code uses `log(K-2)`. AdaBoost is broken for binary targets and mis-weighted for multi-class. [bug] Fix: `double k = firstTargetLevels().size();` and `Math.log(k - 1.0)`; guard `err == 0`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:187,194` `corePredict` loops `for (j = 1; j < varCount; j++)` over the density frame whose columns are the real classes (no `"?"` column), so class index 0 is excluded from `total`, never chosen as `best`, and is not normalized. [bug] Fix: start at `j = 0`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:149-157` when `err == 0`, `alpha = +Infinity` is stored before the `err < eps` early exit; the model then predicts `NaN` densities. [bug] Fix: cap alpha (e.g. `log((1-eps)/eps)`).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:150` `stopOnError` check happens after `alpha` is computed with `err >= 1 - 1/k` (negative or NaN log) but before the learner is stored: fine, yet the learner from a wasted round is discarded without any hook notification. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:172` `ClassifierResult.build(this, df, withClasses, true)` always allocates densities even when `withDistributions == false`, and `withClasses == false` NPEs at line 197 (`firstClasses()` is null). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:116` `weights.darray().div_(sum).dv()` creates a normalized copy `w` and then mutates it in place at line 162/165 — fine — but `Var w` mutation via `setDouble` plus `darray_().div_` mixes two APIs on the same buffer. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:69` `eps` default written `10e-10` (= 1e-9) while GBTRegressionModel uses `1e-10`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:63` weak learner param is named `model`, GBTRegressionModel names it `model` but the param string is `"nodeModel"`, GBTClassifierModel uses `model` typed `RTree`; CForest/RForest use `model` too. Param string mismatch shows in `fullName()`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:104-110` `getAlphas()/getLearners()` expose internal mutable lists. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:128-129` `initModel.get()` is fitted **in place**: the parameter object itself becomes the fitted init model, so `newInstance()` copies share it, `fullName()` changes after fit, and two GBT models sharing one `initModel` instance overwrite each other. [bug] Fix: `initModel.get().newInstance().fit(...)` and store it in a field.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:131,168` loop runs `i = 1..runs` and reports `RunInfo(this, i)` 1-based, while AdaBoost/GBTClassifier/CForest report 0-based. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:141-150` the tree is fitted on the *sampled* rows `xmLearn` but `tree.fit(xmLearn, "target")` drops the sample `weights` (uses unit weights), while the sampler was given `weights`. Then `boostUpdate` uses the sample too. Sample weights are ignored by the whole boosting loop. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:159-167` a round whose score does not improve is silently dropped (`trees.add` skipped) but the loop continues with the same `fitValues`, `gradient` and RNG: with `rowSampler = identity` every following round recomputes the identical tree and is dropped too, so `runs` iterations are wasted. `Math.abs(initScore - nextScore) < eps` also stops on a *worse* step of tiny size. [bug/design] Fix: break when no improvement with identity sampler, or always add (standard GBM).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:168` `runningHook.get().accept(...)` unguarded while other models null-check; `stoppingHook` is never consulted anywhere in boost/ensemble. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:136` unchecked cast of `model.get().newInstance()` to `GBTRtree<...>`; `newInstance()` returning `M` would remove it. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:82-83` the generic type `GBTRtree<? extends RegressionModel<?,?,?>, ? extends RegressionResult, ?>` is repeated 4 times; a type alias via a nested interface would help. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:65` `shrinkage` validated to `(0,1]` here, `Double::isFinite` only in AdaBoost and GBTClassifierModel (negative shrinkage allowed). [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java:78-81` `K`, `f`, `p`, `residual` are fields written during fit (training-set sized matrices) and kept alive after fit; `corePredict` only needs `K` and `trees`. Memory retained and serialized with the model. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java:154` `residual = yk.sub(p)` allocates a new K x n matrix every round although the field was preallocated. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java:166-167` tree is fitted on the sampled rows with `sample.weights()` but `boostUpdate` is then called on the **full** `df` with `yk`/`p` for all rows: leaf values are estimated on rows that were not in the tree's sample (inconsistent with GBTRegressionModel which uses the sample for both). Also `w` weights are ignored in `boostUpdate`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java:163` the temporary target name `"##tt##"` collides silently if an input has that name; GBTRegressionModel uses `"target"` (very likely to collide with a real column). [bug] Fix: generate a unique name or use a positional API.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java:75-76` `model` param has no validator; `RTree` with `loss` L2 is required for the K-deviance update to make sense but nothing enforces `loss`. `debug` parameter (line 70) is never read. [dead]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java:130-135` no early stopping (`eps`), no `stoppingHook`; unlike GBTRegressionModel. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java:202` `if (t != 0)` leaves the density row all zeros (never happens since softmax terms > 0) and the class loop then picks index 0; harmless dead guard. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java:228,238` `trees.get(0).size()` reported as "fitted trees" although there are `K * runs` trees. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRtree.java:34` abstract class with one abstract method and no state: should be an interface (`BoostableRegressor`) so a model can be boostable without inheriting from it. [design]
- Boost tests: `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoostTest.java:56-73` only asserts learner count and `toString`; no accuracy check, which is why the `log(k-1)` bug is invisible. `GBTClassifierModelTest` likewise only checks tree counts and strings. [test] Fix: assert training accuracy on spambase/iris exceeds a threshold and that densities sum to 1.

#### ensemble (CForest, RForest, BaggingMode)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/BaggingMode.java:46` `HARD_VOTE`: `densities.setDouble(i, best, densities.getDouble(i, best))` is a no-op (should add 1). Densities stay all zero, `normalize()` of a zero vector leaves zeros/NaN and `findBestIndex()` returns 0, so HARD_VOTE always predicts the first class. `BaggingModeTest.votingTest` (`/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/BaggingModeTest.java:73-74`) expects `"a"`, which is index 0, so the test passes by accident. [bug] Fix: `densities.setDouble(i, best, densities.getDouble(i, best) + 1)`; make the test expect a non-zero index winner.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/BaggingMode.java:42,64,111` `ClassWeights classWeights` parameter is accepted and ignored by both modes; `CForest.classWeights` (`CForest.java:115`) is therefore a dead parameter. Test has `// TODO: improve testing for class weights`. [dead]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/BaggingMode.java:58,90` `classes.setDouble(i, dv.findBestIndex())` writes a class index through the double API of a nominal var (`rint` then `setInt`). Works, but `setInt` is the intent. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/BaggingMode.java:72-78` `SOFT_VOTE` divides by `t` (row sum of a tree's density) which is 0 for a tree that returned an all-zero row (e.g. CTree node with no rows); produces NaN for the whole ensemble row. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/RForest.java:56-73` `newBagging()` and `newRF()` do not set `rowSampler`, and `RegressionModel.rowSampler` defaults to `identity()`. CForest's constructor sets `bootstrap()`. So an `RForest.newRF()` trains every tree on the full training set; the only randomness is `VarSelector.auto()` and `Splitter.Random`; `newBagging()` (all variables, identity sampler) trains `runs` identical trees (same data, per-tree seeds only affect tie-breaks). [bug] Fix: `rowSampler.set(RowSampler.bootstrap())` in the RForest constructor as CForest does; the factory names promise bagging.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/RForest.java:85` field typed `Param<...>` while every other model exposes `ValueParam<...>`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/RForest.java:133` and `CForest.java:376` `newInstance().seed.set(seed)` with `seed = random.nextLong()`: if the drawn long is `0L` the weak learner falls back to an unseeded RNG (both `getRandom` implementations treat 0 as "no seed"). Probability negligible, but the "0 means unset" convention is the root cause; use `Optional`/`null` for unset. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/RForest.java:115-124` and `CForest.java:240-264` identical parallel scaffolding (seed array, work-stealing pool, `ParallelStreamCollector.streamingOrdered`, `WeakPredictorInfo` record, `shutdownNow`). Duplicate; the pool is created per fit and `shutdownNow()` is not in a `finally`, so an exception in a tree leaks the pool threads. [design/bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/RForest.java:118-119` weak learner is an `RTree` whose `learnNode` itself uses `.parallel()` on the common pool while the forest runs on its own work-stealing pool: nested parallelism, `poolSize` does not bound total threads. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/RForest.java:144-146` predicts trees with `parallelStream()` (common pool, ignores `poolSize`), CForest predicts sequentially (`CForest.java:386-390`). [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/RForest.java:153-155` `buildComplete()` only when `withResiduals`, while every other regressor calls it unconditionally (it is itself a no-op without residuals). [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/RForest.java:122` and `CForest.java:262` `runningHook` is invoked, `stoppingHook` is never consulted in either forest (nor in any boosting model). A user setting `stoppingHook` gets no effect. [bug] Fix: honour it or remove the base-class parameter.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:105-108` default `model` is a `CTree` with `rowSampler.set(RowSampler.bootstrap())` — CTree ignores `rowSampler`; the forest's own sampler (constructor, line 129) is what matters. Misleading configuration that also prints in `fullName()`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:90,95,100,113` param strings `"freqVI"`, `"gainVI"`, `"viPerm"`, `"bagging"` vs fields `viFreq`, `viGain`, `viPerm`, `baggingMode`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:163-168` `capabilities()` declares `targets(... NOMINAL, BINARY)` although the default CTree (and every learner in the package) accepts only NOMINAL targets; a BINARY target passes the forest check and fails inside the first tree. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:361-368` OOB error counts every training row, including rows that were never out-of-bag (all-zero density row, `argmax()` = 0, compared against the true class). With few trees this inflates the OOB error and biases it toward class 0. [bug] Fix: skip rows whose OOB density sum is 0 and divide by the number of scored rows.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:347-370` `oobCompute` is called after each tree and recomputes the argmax over the whole training set every time: O(runs * n * K) instead of once at the end. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:284` permutation importance uses `Var.shuffle()`, which calls `SamplingTools.sampleWOR(size, size)` with `new Random()` (`/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/data/Var.java:528`): permutation VI is not reproducible under `seed`. [bug] Fix: `SamplingTools.sampleWOR(new Random(seed+run), ...)`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:205-206` permutation VI statistics: `zscore = mean / sd` should be `mean / (sd / sqrt(n))` for a mean over `n` trees, and `pvalue = normal.cdf(2 * normal.cdf(-|z|))` wraps a probability in another CDF; the two-sided p-value is `2 * normal.cdf(-|z|)`. Reported p-values are wrong. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:452` "Permutation Variable Importance" section prints `getGainVIInfo()` instead of `getPermVIInfo()`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:180-181` `maxScore == 0` gives NaN scaled scores; `getVIInfo` sorts by `score.refComparator(false)` on the unsorted `score` var then `.copy()` — fine, but VI maps are `HashMap` so row order before sorting is arbitrary and ties are unstable. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:384` `corePredict` ignores `withClasses`/`withDensities` and always builds both. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:305,329` variable importance is only computed when the weak learner `instanceof CTree`; silently produces empty maps for other learners while `viFreq/viGain` stay `true` and `toFullContent` prints empty tables. [design] Fix: validate at fit or expose a `VariableImportanceProvider` interface.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:118-126` `predictors` is reassigned per fit while `RForest.regressions` is `final` and cleared; `oobDensities` and VI maps are training-set sized and serialized with the model. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:132-150` `predictors()`, `oobDensities()` etc. expose mutable internals. [nit]
- Ensemble tests: `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/RForestTest.java` never asserts that different trees differ or that RMSE improves with more trees (would have caught the missing bootstrap); `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForestTest.java` does not test `HARD_VOTE` accuracy, OOB error bounds, or permutation-VI reproducibility under seed. [test]

#### rule (OneRule, ZeroRule, ManualClassifier, onerule.*)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/onerule/HolteBinning.java:83` inner loop over the *sorted index array* `rows` reads the target with `df.getLabel(j, ...)` (raw position `j`) and the weight with `weights.getDouble(rows[i])` (the bin's first row) instead of `rows[j]` for both. Each bin's class histogram is built from the wrong rows, so numeric 1R rules are learned on scrambled labels. [bug] Fix: `df.getLabel(rows[j], target)` and `weights.getDouble(rows[j])`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/onerule/HolteBinning.java:131-134` missing-value rule: the loop variable `j` is never used (`rows[i]` is read `df.rowCount()-len` times), and `hist` is created with `emptyByLabels(true, levels)` (has a leading `"?"` slot) but incremented by `df.getInt(...)` which is the 0-based real-class index, so every count lands one slot too early (class 0 counted as `"?"`). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/onerule/HolteBinning.java:55-65` relies on `refComparator()` sorting missing values last (`break` on first missing); if missing sort first `len == 0` and no numeric rule is produced. Not asserted anywhere. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/OneRule.java:155-156` `MissingHandler.MAJORITY` returns `Pair.from(testVar, missingDensity)`: the *variable name* is used as the predicted label and written with `setLabel`, which adds a bogus level to the prediction var. Should be `missingDensity.findBestLabel()`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/OneRule.java:143` `for (int j = 1; j < targetLevels.size(); j++)` skips the density of the first class (levels have no `"?"`). Same off-by-one as `CTree.predictPoint`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/OneRule.java:182` fallback returns label `"?"` (missing) and an all-zero density; a row that matches no rule is silently predicted as missing instead of the majority class. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/OneRule.java:112-115` `BINARY` inputs go through `buildNominal`, which indexes `densityVectors[df.getInt(i, testIndex) + 1]` and builds `testDict` from `levels()`; correctness depends on `VarBinary.levels()`/`getLabel()` agreeing with `getInt()` ordering, which is not asserted (no binary-input test in `OneRuleTest`). [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/OneRule.java:116` best rule set chosen by `>` on accuracy: ties resolve to the first input in column order; `RuleSet.getAccuracy()` is NaN when total weight is 0 and then never wins. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/OneRule.java:163,175` unchecked downcasts `(NumericRule)`/`(NominalRule)` decided by the *predict* frame's column type rather than by the rule type stored at fit; a frame whose column type changed between fit and predict throws `ClassCastException`. [design] Fix: dispatch on `rule instanceof`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/OneRule.java:60,130` `java.util.logging` `log.severe` immediately followed by throwing the same message: double reporting. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/ZeroRule.java:61-63` `newInstance()` returns `new ZeroRule()` without `copyParameterValues`, unlike every other model (base params `runs`, `seed`, hooks are lost). [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/ZeroRule.java:75` capabilities allow BINARY targets and missing target values, but `DensityVector.fromLevelWeights(false, ...)` ignores missing and `setDouble(i, prediction, 1)` addresses a density column by label; untested for BINARY. [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/ManualClassifier.java:39` no `serialVersionUID`; factory is `model()` while every sibling uses `newModel()`; `newInstance()` return type is the abstract `ClassifierModel<...>` instead of `ManualClassifier`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/ManualClassifier.java:75-79` majority label computed from unweighted `Unique` counts: `weights` ignored. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/ManualClassifier.java:45-46` `ListParam` validator `(list, list2) -> true` and no null check on rule results; a rule returning a label not in the target dictionary silently adds a level on predict. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/onerule/Rule.java:46` `zeroWeight = |sum| < 1e-32`; `getAcc()` returns 0 for empty rules so an empty bin is treated as 0% accurate rather than excluded. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/rule/onerule/RuleSet.java:42-43` package-private fields with a public mutable `getRules()` list. [nit]
- Tests: `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/model/rule/onerule/HolteBinningTest.java` passes with the `rows[j]` bug, which means it does not use a numeric column whose sorted order differs from row order (or only checks bin boundaries, not class histograms). #### bayes (NaiveBayes, nb.*)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinomialEstimator.java:170` `fit(...)` always `return false` after successfully fitting, and `NaiveBayes.coreFit` (`NaiveBayes.java:154-158`) throws `IllegalStateException("... cannot be fitted")` on `false`. A NaiveBayes with a Multinomial estimator can never be fitted. [bug] Fix: `return true`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/BernoulliEstimator.java:117-123` table rows are *test* levels and columns *target* levels, then `normalizeOnRows()` normalizes each test level across targets, i.e. it stores `P(target | test)` instead of the likelihood `P(test | target)` that Naive Bayes multiplies. `MultinoulliEstimator.fitNominal` correctly uses `normalizeOnCols()`. `BernoulliEstimatorTest.testFit` (`/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/BernoulliEstimatorTest.java:100-104`) asserts the wrong direction (`P(a | x=0) = 0.5`). [bug] Fix: `normalizeOnCols()` and fix the test expectations.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/BernoulliEstimator.java:117-121` `fromLabels(true, ...)` adds a `"?"` *target* column and Laplace-smooths it, although targets cannot be missing; the phantom column takes probability mass from the real classes (test expects `[?:0.333, 0:0.5, 1:0.5]`). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/BernoulliEstimator.java:117`, `GaussianEstimator.java:122-130`, `KernelEstimator.java:124-126`, `MultinoulliEstimator.java:157,191`, `MultinomialEstimator.java:160`, `PoissonEstimator.java:126-127`: every estimator ignores the `weights` argument (`fromLabels(..., null)`, unweighted `OnlineStat`, `inc(..., 1)`), while `PriorMLE` is weighted. `NaiveBayes.fit(df, weights, ...)` silently drops sample weights for likelihoods. [bug] Fix: pass weights everywhere or drop the parameter from `Estimator.fit`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:171-176` posterior computed as `exp(sum of logs)` per class with no max-shift; with a few dozen features the sum underflows to `exp(-800) = 0` for all classes, `normalize()` divides by 0 and the prediction degenerates to class 0 / NaN densities. [bug] Fix: subtract `max_j sumLog_j` before `exp`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:149-158` `prior.get()` and each `Estimator` in `estimators.get()` are fitted **in place**: the parameter objects hold the fitted state, `newInstance().copyParameterValues(this)` shares the same estimator instances, and two model copies (e.g. in CV or CStacking) overwrite each other's likelihood tables. `Estimator.newInstance()`/`Prior.newInstance()` exist but are never called. [bug] Fix: clone estimators in `coreFit` and store the fitted copies in a field.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:167` prediction uses `IntStream.parallel()` on the common pool, ignoring `poolSize`; `VarNominal.setLabel` from multiple threads is only safe because every label is already in the dictionary. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:118-142` overrides `prepareFit` and re-implements target parsing, but `targetLevels` is filled only for the first target and `capabilities()` is checked *after* the custom validation; `estimators` order defines `inputNames`, so `inputVarsSummary` differs from the frame order used by every other model. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:105-108` `fullName()` overridden to print *fitted* state (`fittedName()`), so `fullName()` changes after `fit` and `equalOnParams`-style comparisons between a fitted and unfitted copy fail; base params (`seed`, `runs`) are omitted. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:114` capabilities accept `BINARY` targets but `df.rvar(target).levels()` for a `VarBinary` and `firstTargetLevel(j)` labels must match the estimators' `df.getLabel` strings; not tested. [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/GaussianEstimator.java:131-137` `stats.get(label)` is null when a target level has no rows in the (sub)sample → NPE; `Normal.of(mu, sd)` with `sd == 0` (constant feature within a class) either throws or gives a degenerate pdf that `predict` special-cases with `1e-20` thresholds. Standard fix is variance smoothing (`var + eps * max var`). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/GaussianEstimator.java:132` and `KernelEstimator.java:121` `"?".equals(label)` checks are dead: `levels()` never contains `"?"`. [dead]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/GaussianEstimator.java:144-150` `normals.get(targetLevel)` looked up twice; null for unknown level → NPE while the other estimators return `NaN`/`eps`/`0`. Also `hasLearned` is set here but not in `KernelEstimator`/`PoissonEstimator`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/KernelEstimator.java:119-127` fits KDEs with `parallelStream()` over target levels and one full-frame `stream().filter()` per level; `bandwidth == 0` means "Silverman", so a genuine bandwidth of 0 cannot be expressed and a constant feature yields bandwidth 0 → NaN pdf. `predict` returns `NaN` for unknown level, which then poisons `log`. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinoulliEstimator.java:71` `int ourCase` with a comment legend instead of an enum; `predict` returns `NaN` for an unknown target level while `Bernoulli` returns `0.0`, `Poisson` returns `eps`, `Multinomial` returns `NaN`. Four conventions for the same situation, and `NaiveBayes` handles none of them (`log(NaN)`). [inconsistency] Fix: define in `Estimator` Javadoc that `predict` returns a strictly positive likelihood or throws.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinoulliEstimator.java:144-152` `validate` scans the whole frame to check binary columns sum to ≤ 1 at every `fit`, then `fitBinary` throws if no column is 1 (rows of all zeros are rejected although `sum <= 1` allowed them). [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinoulliEstimator.java:87` typo `"lapaceSmoother"` in `name()`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinomialEstimator.java:93-94` `public static final double eps`, `DEFAULT_LAPLACE` lower-case constant name; factories named `fromNames/fromRange` while every other estimator uses `forName/forNames/forRange`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinomialEstimator.java:203-215` full multinomial pmf including `lnGamma(n+1)` and `-lnGamma(x_i+1)`; these terms are identical for all classes and only add numeric risk; returns `eps` when `!isFinite`, hiding overflow. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/PoissonEstimator.java:117` integer check `Math.abs(value - rint(value)) > 1e-100` is effectively `!=`; a fractional-but-tiny difference fails silently with `LOGGER.fine` and `return false`, which NaiveBayes turns into an exception with a generic message. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/PoissonEstimator.java:71,82` `getLambdaMap()` returns the internal `HashMap`; `lambdaMap` is never cleared on refit. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/PoissonEstimator.java:146-148` missing test value is treated as `x = 0` instead of being skipped (contributing likelihood 1). [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/PriorUniform.java:42,68` `levels` set is never cleared between fits, so refitting on a target with fewer levels keeps stale levels; `df.levels(targetVar)` computed three times. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/AbstractEstimator.java:31-45` `hasLearned` is a protected field that no caller reads (`Estimator` has no `isFitted()`); `getTestNames()` returns the mutable internal list. [dead]
- Tests: `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayesTest.java` does not exercise `MultinomialEstimator` inside `NaiveBayes.fit` (would fail immediately), sample weights, or a many-feature dataset where underflow appears. [test]

#### knn (KnnRegression)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:165` `w.clamp_(1 - eps.get(), eps.get())`: `DArray.clamp_(min, max)` (`/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/darray/DArray.java:1544`, applied as "if v < min set min; if v > max set max") is called with `min = 1-eps > max = eps`, so every normalized distance first becomes `1-eps` and then `eps`. All neighbour weights collapse to `kernel(eps)`, a constant: every kernel degenerates to a plain unweighted k-NN mean. [bug] Fix: `w.clamp_(eps.get(), 1 - eps.get())`. `KnnRegressionTest.overfitTest` uses `k=1`, where any weighting is a no-op, so it cannot detect this. [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:136-146` `computeTop` uses a `TreeSet<Integer>` whose comparator compares *distances only*: two instances at the same distance compare equal and the second is silently dropped from the set (`TreeSet` treats compare==0 as duplicate). With duplicated inputs (common in INT/BINARY features) fewer than `k` neighbours are returned and `indexes[]` is padded with `0` (instance 0). Also `distance.compute` is re-evaluated inside the comparator, O(n log k) distance computations per query with no caching. [bug] Fix: precompute distances once, sort indices by `(distance, index)`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:179-181` uses the `(k+1)`-th neighbour as the bandwidth reference; if `n <= k` `computeTop` returns padded zeros and `wref` may be 0 (division by zero → Inf → clamped). `coreFit` only checks `rowCount() < 2`. [bug] Fix: validate `rowCount() > k`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:81` `wdistance` parameter is declared, printed and copied but never used (`computeWeights` uses `distance`). [dead]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:123-132` `weights` (sample weights) are ignored. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:62` no `serialVersionUID` unlike every other model; `DArray<Double>[] instances` generic array with `@SuppressWarnings("unchecked")`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:204` enum constant `TRIANGLUAR` (typo, public API). [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:192-196` `INV` kernel `1/v` with `v` clamped away from 0 by `eps`; `COS` kernel constant `PI * cos(v * HALF_PI) / 4` is a density normalization irrelevant after `/ weights.sum()`. Kernel `k` argument only used by `GAUSSIAN`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:109-110` capabilities allow INT/LONG/BINARY targets but `target.darray_()` and `RegressionResult` assume double; no classification counterpart (`KnnClassifier`) exists in the package. [design]

#### simple (L1Regression, L2Regression, ConstantRegression, RandomValueRegression)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/simple/L2Regression.java:78` and `L1Regression.java:92` mean/median are computed **unweighted** although `fit(df, weights, ...)` accepts weights and `GBTRegressionModel` uses `L2Regression` as `initModel` with sample weights. `Mean`/`Quantiles` have weighted variants. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/simple/L2Regression.java:58-60` and `L1Regression.java:67-69` `newInstance()` returns a bare `new X()` without `copyParameterValues` (drops `seed`, `runs`, hooks), unlike `ConstantRegression`/`RandomValueRegression`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/simple/L2Regression.java:171-173` `equalOnParams` returns `true` unconditionally and `ParametricEquals` is implemented only here and in `CTree`/`RVMRegression`; not a consistent contract. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/simple/L1Regression.java:57` factory `newL1()` vs `L2Regression.newModel()`, `ConstantRegression.with(c)`, `RandomValueRegression.newRVR()/from(d)`: four naming styles in one package. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/simple/L2Regression.java:70-71` capabilities: targets `(1, 1_000_000, allowMissing=true)` but `Mean.of` on a var with missing yields the mean of non-missing (fine) while `RegressionResult.buildComplete` computes residuals with NaN targets and NaN `tss`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/simple/L2Regression.java:121-163` `toContent` and `toFullContent` are identical copies (same in `L1Regression`), and variable `median` holds means. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/simple/RandomValueRegression.java:91` `getRandom()` is called at every `predict`, so two predictions on the same frame with a fixed seed return identical "random" values and different frames restart the stream; also the `Distribution` parameter has no `newInstance` and may itself be stateful. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/simple/ConstantRegression.java:53` `constant` validator `x -> true` accepts `null`, then `s.setDouble(constant.get())` NPEs on predict. [nit] Fix: `Objects::nonNull`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/simple/*` `capabilities()` list `VarType.STRING` inputs although `Capabilities` for every real learner excludes it; harmless because inputs are ignored, but a stacking/CV harness that intersects capabilities gets STRING. [nit]

#### meta (CStacking)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java:114` vs `:132` the stacker is **fitted** on density columns `j = 1..K-1` of each weak learner but **predicted** with columns `j = 0..K-1`: the prediction frame has `W` extra columns (named `<level0>_i`) that were never seen at fit and, for the default column-position-based learners, shifts all features. Result: the stack model predicts on misaligned/unknown inputs. [bug] Fix: use the same range in both (dropping one redundant column is fine, but do it in both places).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java:111-113` weak learners are fitted and then predicted **on the same training frame**; classic stacking uses out-of-fold predictions, otherwise the stacker learns from overfitted (near-perfect) weak densities. No CV, no comment. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java:75` `fullName()` returns `"CStacking{stacker=...;"` (unbalanced brace, learners omitted), and the parameter is named `"stacker"` while the field is `stackModel`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java:102` `learned = false;` written inside `coreFit` whose return value is what sets `learned` afterwards; dead assignment. [dead]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java:110,119` every weak learner and the stacker receive the *same* `seed`; with several identical learner types (e.g. two CForests) they become identical models. [design] Fix: derive per-learner seeds from a `Random(seed)`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java:57-58` `ListParam` validator `(__, ___) -> true`; `stackModel` default `null` with `Objects::nonNull` validator means `set(null)` is rejected but the default is null, so `validateParams` is needed at fit. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java:81` capabilities exclude NOMINAL inputs although weak learners such as CTree/NaiveBayes accept them; the meta model is more restrictive than its parts for no reason. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java:136` `corePredict` ignores `withClasses/withDistributions` (always predicts both from the stacker) and `ClassifierResult.copy` shares the stacker's `Var`s. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java:55,99-112` `java.util.logging` `fine` messages with string concatenation in the hot path; no other model logs. [nit]
- Test `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStackingTest.java` (68 lines) does not compare fit-time and predict-time feature frames or accuracy; the column mismatch is undetected. [test]

#### Cross-cutting test gaps

- No test in `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/model` asserts that **sample weights** change a fitted model (trees, forests, boosting, NB, kNN, simple all accept weights; several ignore them). A test that duplicates rows vs doubles weights and asserts equal predictions would catch Search.Binary, GBT, NB estimators, L1/L2, kNN, ManualClassifier. [test]
- No test asserts **seed reproducibility** for RTree/RForest/CForest (two fits with the same seed → identical predictions); would catch the `.parallel()` shared `Random` in RTree and `Var.shuffle()` in permutation VI. [test]
- No test exercises **missing values at predict time** through a nominal binary split (`NominalNotEqual` routing) or through `CTree.predictPoint`'s fallback (index-0 normalization bug). [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModelSerializationTest.java` covers classifiers only; there is no regression-model serialization test, which is why the non-serializable `DEFAULT_STOPPING_HOOK`/`Consumer` hooks in `RegressionModel` go unnoticed. [test]
- Many tests assert `toString()`/`fullName()` strings (RForestTest, GBT tests, AdaBoostTest, estimator tests) rather than behaviour; these break on any parameter rename yet pass with wrong math. [test]

#### Summary (top 5)

1. **AdaBoost is broken for binary targets** (`AdaBoost.java:117,149`): `k = levels-1` then `log(k-1)` = `log(0)` → alpha = -Infinity for every round; also `corePredict` skips class index 0 (`:187,194`). Tests only check counts/strings.
2. **Off-by-one on class index 0** in three places that assume a leading `"?"` level that `VarNominal.levels()` no longer has: `CTree.predictPoint` (`CTree.java:345`, missing-value fallback), `OneRule.corePredict` (`OneRule.java:143`), `BaggingMode.HARD_VOTE` no-op increment (`BaggingMode.java:46`, always predicts class 0); plus `DensityTable.hasColsWithMinimumCount` skipping column 0 (used by every ctree Search). Add a shared test on a 3-class dataset checking densities sum to 1 and class 0 is predictable.
3. **Sample weights silently ignored** in `Search.Binary`, `rtree.Node.boostUpdate`, `GBTRegressionModel` tree fit, all NB estimators, `L1/L2Regression`, `KnnRegression`, `ManualClassifier`; and **weights direction wrong** in `BernoulliEstimator` (`normalizeOnRows` = P(target|test)); **`MultinomialEstimator.fit` always returns false**, so it cannot be used in NaiveBayes at all.
4. **Ensembles/boosting configuration bugs**: `RForest` has no bootstrap by default (identity `rowSampler`, `RForest.java:56-73`), `RTree` uses a shared `Random` inside `.parallel()` (no seed reproducibility, `RTree.java:241`), `GBTRegressionModel` fits its `initModel` parameter in place (`:128`), `CStacking` fits on `K-1` density columns but predicts with `K` (`CStacking.java:114/132`), `KnnRegression.clamp_` argument order makes every kernel uniform (`KnnRegression.java:165`), `HolteBinning` reads labels from unsorted row positions (`HolteBinning.java:83`).
5. **Design debt**: `CTree`/`RTree` and their `Node/Candidate/Search/Splitter` are parallel copies with drifted semantics (depth convention, `minCount` meaning, `Majority` splitter, missing-value handling in `NominalNotEqual`), `ClassifierModel`/`RegressionModel` duplicate the fit pipeline with different defaults (`poolSize`, `getRandom`, hook types, `stopHook` name, non-serializable regression hooks), and `stoppingHook`/`classWeights`/`maxSize`/`wdistance`/`debug`/`missingPenalty`(nominal) are declared parameters that nothing reads.

### 2.4 ml.model bayes, rule, knn, simple, meta

# Review: rapaio ml/model/{bayes,rule,knn,simple,meta}

#### bayes (NaiveBayes + nb estimators/priors)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/BernoulliEstimator.java:123` [bug] `normalizeOnRows()` normalizes each test-level row across target levels, i.e. it stores P(target | x) not the likelihood P(x | target). `MultinoulliEstimator` correctly uses `normalizeOnCols()`. In the NB product this multiplies the class prior in once per Bernoulli feature and biases the argmax. `BernoulliEstimatorTest.testFit:108` cements the wrong numbers (`{targetLevel:a,[0:0.5,1:0.5]}` is P(a|x)). Fix: `normalizeOnCols()` and fix the test expectations.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/BernoulliEstimator.java:117-121` [bug] `fromLabels(true, ...)` adds a `?` row AND a `?` target column, and Laplace mass is added to those too, so per-class likelihoods leak probability into a "missing target" column that is never predicted. Use `withMissing=false` for the target axis (or build the table with `DensityTable.empty`).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinomialEstimator.java:170` [bug] `fit()` always returns `false`, so `NaiveBayes.coreFit:155` throws "Estimator ... cannot be fitted" for every model using it. The estimator is unusable inside `NaiveBayes`. `MultinomialEstimatorTest` never asserts the return value nor uses the estimator through `NaiveBayes`. Fix: `return true;`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:171-178` [bug] log-space sum is exponentiated per class without subtracting the max (`Math.exp(sumLog)`); with many features all classes underflow to 0 and `dv.normalize()` divides by zero → NaN densities / arbitrary class. Use log-sum-exp (subtract max log before `exp`).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:114` [bug] `capabilities()` accepts a BINARY target, but `firstTargetLevels()` for `VarBinary` is `["true","false"]` (VarBinary.java:380) while `IndexLabel.fromVarLevels` (used by PriorMLE / DensityTable) keys binary levels as `"0","1"`. `computePrior("true")` → NaN → every prediction NaN. Either drop BINARY from targets or unify level naming.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:96,149,153` [bug] `newInstance()` copies parameter *references*: `ListParam.copyFrom` (ListParam.java:134) stores the same `Estimator` objects, and `ValueParam` the same `Prior`. `coreFit` then mutates those shared objects in place, so two models created via `newInstance()` (e.g. in CV/ensembles) overwrite each other's fitted state and are not thread-safe. Fit `estimator.newInstance()`/`prior.newInstance()` copies and keep them in model fields.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:174` [bug] estimators disagree on the "unknown target level" return value: Bernoulli `0.0`, Poisson `eps`, Multinoulli/Kernel/Multinomial `NaN`, PriorMLE/PriorUniform `NaN`. `log(NaN)` poisons the whole density vector (all classes NaN after `normalize()`). Define one contract (return a small epsilon) in `Estimator` Javadoc and enforce it.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:113` [bug] inputs declared `allowMissing=true`, but no numeric estimator handles missing test values: `GaussianEstimator.fit:129` feeds NaN into `OnlineStat` (mean/sd become NaN for that class), `KernelEstimator.fit:125` builds a KDE over NaNs, `MultinomialEstimator.fit:160` increments counts with NaN. Skip `df.isMissing(i, testName)` rows in fit and return a neutral value (1.0) in predict.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/GaussianEstimator.java:135` [bug] `stats.get(label)` is null (NPE) when a declared target level has no training rows.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/GaussianEstimator.java:136` [design] uses population variance (`OnlineStat.variance = m2/n`, ddof=0); scikit-learn/Weka use ddof=0 too, so acceptable, but it should be documented; with a single sample sd=0 and the Dirac branch at :147 then returns hard 0/1 which zeroes the class entirely. Add a variance floor (`var_smoothing`, like sklearn's 1e-9·max var).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/GaussianEstimator.java:132` `KernelEstimator.java:121` [dead code] `"?".equals(label)` skip is unreachable: `VarNominal.levels()` never contains `?` (VarNominal.java:339-347).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/GaussianEstimator.java:146,150` [nit] mixes `getTestNames().get(0)` and `testName`; `normals.get(targetLevel)` looked up twice.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/KernelEstimator.java:124-126` [bug] a target level with no rows produces an empty `Var` → `KDE.silvermanBandwidth` on empty data (NaN / exception). Also `fit` returns `true` without checking that `testVarName` exists or is numeric, unlike Gaussian/Bernoulli.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/KernelEstimator.java:57,64` [inconsistency] `forRange/forType` return `List<Estimator>` while Gaussian/Bernoulli/Poisson return `List<Specific>`; `newInstance()` return type is `Estimator` in Gaussian/Kernel/Multinomial but covariant in Bernoulli/Multinoulli/Poisson.

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinomialEstimator.java:131-133` [bug] `fittedName()` iterates `targetLevels` (all declared levels) but `densityMap` only has levels present in training → NPE for a level without rows.

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinomialEstimator.java:197-199` [design] allocates a `DArray` per row per class in `predict` (called from a parallel stream). A plain `double[]` or direct reads from the frame would avoid the allocations.

Return a neutral likelihood instead.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinoulliEstimator.java:144-152,188-190` [inconsistency] `validate` only rejects rows whose binary sum > 1 (message says "does not sum to 1") but `fitBinary` then throws for rows summing to 0; and `Math.abs(getDouble)` on a missing binary is NaN so the check passes silently. Validate `sum == 1` exactly once.

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/PoissonEstimator.java:126-137` [bug] `lambdaMap` is never cleared before refit (unlike `GaussianEstimator.normals.clear()`), so stale levels survive a second `fit` on a different frame.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/PoissonEstimator.java:82,144-145` [design] `getLambdaMap()` exposes the mutable internal map; predicting missing as `pdf(0)` is an arbitrary imputation, and the `Poisson.of(1e-20)` sentinel for zero-mean classes is undocumented.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/PriorUniform.java:42,68` [bug] `levels` is a `final HashSet` that is never cleared in `fitPriors`; refitting on a frame with different target levels accumulates levels while `uniformPrior` is recomputed from the new frame only.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/PriorMLE.java:65` [design] no smoothing: a level with zero total weight gets prior 0 → `log(0) = -inf` → class can never be predicted, even if every likelihood favours it.

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/Estimator.java:65` [inconsistency] `fit(df, weights, target)` receives instance weights but every estimator ignores them (only `PriorMLE` uses them). Either implement weighted counts or drop the parameter / document it.
