#### ml.loss

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/L1LossFunction.java:49-51` [bug] `scalarMinimizer(Var y, Var weight)` ignores `weight` and returns the unweighted median. Should be the weighted median.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/HuberLossFunction.java:82-84` [bug] Same: weighted `scalarMinimizer` delegates to the unweighted one, so any boosting/tree code passing weights gets a wrong leaf value silently.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/HuberLossFunction.java:154,168` [bug] `alpha` has two meanings. In `scalarMinimizer`/`additiveScalarMinimizer`/`gradient` it is the *quantile* used to derive the threshold `rho` (Friedman's formulation); in `error()` and `residualErrorScore()` it is used directly as the *threshold* `delta` (`a < alpha`). With the default `alpha = 0.25` the reported error is Huber loss with delta=0.25 regardless of residual scale, inconsistent with the gradient. Compute `rho` from the alpha-quantile in `error()` too, or introduce a separate `delta`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/L2LossFunction.java:76-82,86-92` [inconsistency] `errorScore` returns `sqrt(sum sq / (2n))` — neither the sum of `error()` (which is `sq/2` per row, as L1 and Huber do) nor RMSE. L1 and Huber return `sum(error)`. Define `errorScore = sum(error(y, y_hat))` uniformly, or document the L2 special case.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/L2LossFunction.java:64-66,72` [bug] `gradient` uses `y.size()` while `len = min(y.size(), y_hat.size())` is computed and unused (throws when `y_hat` is shorter, silently truncates in `error`). Either validate equal sizes or use `len` consistently.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/LossFunction.java:81` vs `L2LossFunction.java:66`, `L1LossFunction.java:60`, `HuberLossFunction.java:142` [inconsistency] Javadoc says "gradient of the loss", implementations return `y - y_hat` / `sign(y - y_hat)`, i.e. the **negative** gradient (pseudo-residual). Document as pseudo-residual or flip the sign.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/LossFunction.java:47,57,69` [nit] Javadoc formulas say `argmax_{c} sum loss(...)`; a minimizer is `argmin`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/L1LossFunction.java:60` vs `HuberLossFunction.java:74,144` [inconsistency] Subgradient at zero residual is `+1` in L1 and `-1` in Huber.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/L1LossFunction.java:70,75` vs `L2LossFunction.java:79` [inconsistency] L1/Huber use `nanSum()` (skip missing) while L2 propagates NaN.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/HuberLossFunction.java:45-51` [design] `withAlpha` mutates a shared, `Serializable` loss instance in place instead of returning a new one; two models sharing one `HuberLossFunction` can change each other's alpha. Make `alpha` final and return a new instance.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/HuberLossFunction.java:46` [nit] `alpha == 0` passes validation but makes `rho` the smallest absolute residual, so almost every gradient is clipped; `alpha == 1` degenerates to L2. Restrict to (0,1).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/HuberLossFunction.java:88-121` vs `58-79` [inconsistency] `additiveScalarMinimizer` is a copy of `scalarMinimizer` applied to `y - fx`; call `scalarMinimizer(residual)` instead.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/KDevianceLossFunction.java:107-123` [inconsistency] Unsupported methods throw `IllegalStateException` (one without message); should be `UnsupportedOperationException`. Contract violation is invisible at the interface level — consider splitting `LossFunction` so that boosting-only losses do not have to stub four methods.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/KDevianceLossFunction.java:43` [nit] `k` is not validated (`k >= 2`); `k = 1` gives a zero minimizer for every input.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/KDevianceLossFunction.java:53-104` [inconsistency] The three minimizers are near-identical copies with slightly different NaN-guard layouts (one `if` at 61/77, two at 95-101).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/L2LossFunction.java:82,92` [nit] `len == 0` yields `0/0 = NaN` rather than 0 or an exception.
- [test] There is **no** `tst/rapaio/ml/loss` directory: none of the minimizers, gradients or the weighted variants have a unit test (e.g. L2 minimizer == mean, L1 == median, Huber alpha semantics, KDeviance formula against Friedman's paper). This is the largest coverage gap in scope.

#### ml.common.distance

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/distance/MinkowskiDistance.java:39` [bug] `name()` returns `"Euclidean"`; copy-paste. Should be `"Minkowski(p=…)"`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/distance/MinkowskiDistance.java:54,63` [bug] `pow(delta, p)` without `abs`: for negative `delta` and non-integer `p` the result is NaN, for odd integer `p` negative terms cancel positive ones. Must be `pow(abs(delta), p)`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/distance/MinkowskiDistance.java:33` [nit] `p` is not validated (`p >= 1`); `p = 0` gives `1/p = Infinity`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/distance/EuclideanDistance.java:52-58`, `Manhattan.java:42-48`, `MinkowskiDistance.java:58-65` [design] Frame overloads iterate `df1.varNames()` and read `getDouble` on every column: nominal columns contribute their level index, missing values produce NaN, and `df2` is assumed to have the same column names. Restrict to numeric columns and validate, or document that callers must pre-select.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/distance/EuclideanDistance.java:48` [nit] `x.sub(y).apply_(v -> v*v).sum()` allocates a temporary; a fused loop (or `sub(y).sqr_()` if available) avoids it in the k-NN/k-means hot path.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/distance/Manhattan.java:29` vs `EuclideanDistance`, `MinkowskiDistance` [inconsistency] Class naming: `Manhattan` lacks the `Distance` suffix; `Constant` too.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/distance/Distance.java:41` [design] `Distance` is not `Serializable`, yet it is held by `Serializable` models in `ml.model.km` (KMedoids, KMCluster, MWKMeans) — serializing such a model fails or needs a transient hack. `LossFunction` is `Serializable`; make `Distance` consistent.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/distance/Constant.java:35` [nit] `constant(int)` accepts an `int` but the field is a `double`; no `double` overload.
- [test] No tests under `tst/rapaio/ml/common/distance`: Minkowski vs Euclidean (p=2) and vs Manhattan (p=1) equivalence would have caught both Minkowski bugs.

#### ml.common (top level)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/VarSelector.java:62-75` [bug] `withVarNames` mutates `this.mCount` (a side effect on the receiver) and then builds the copy with `new VarSelector(mCount)`, i.e. passes the *computed count* as `mVars`. The copy therefore loses its mode: an `auto()` selector becomes `fixed(ceil(sqrt(n)))`, `all()` with n>0 vars becomes `fixed(n)` (so `nextVarNames` samples without replacement instead of returning all — same set, shuffled order, slower), and `name()` on the copy reports `VarSelector[3]` instead of `VarSelector[AUTO]`. Chaining `withVarNames` twice on a growing var set gives wrong counts. Fix: `new VarSelector(this.mVars)` and drop the mutation.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/VarSelector.java:47,51-52` [bug] `fixed(0)` is indistinguishable from `all()` and `fixed(-1)` from `auto()` because the sentinels share the `int` domain; no validation of `m >= 1`. Use an enum mode + count.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/VarSelector.java:55-56` [design] Mutable non-final fields in a `Serializable` value object; `varNames` array exposed only via copies (good) but the object itself is not immutable.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/ClassWeights.java:56-63` [design] `instanceCount` returns raw class *counts* under a type called `ClassWeights`; a caller expecting balanced weights (inverse frequency) gets the opposite. Rename or add `balanced(Var)`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/ClassWeights.java:35-54,58` [inconsistency] Five hand-written `of(...)` overloads (use a `Map` or varargs pairs), and `getWeights()` returns an immutable `Map.of` for those but a mutable `HashMap` for `instanceCount`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/ClassWeights.java:33` [nit] Not `Serializable` although classifiers holding it are.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/Capabilities.java:172,175` [bug] Target-count error messages print `minInputCount`/`maxInputCount` instead of `minTargetCount`/`maxTargetCount`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/Capabilities.java:161,172` [nit] "requires more than N" when the check is `size < min` (should be "at least N").
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/Capabilities.java:146-236` [nit] `Var weights` parameter is threaded through all six check methods and never used.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/Capabilities.java:205` [nit] `checkInputTypes` is package-private while its five siblings are private.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/Capabilities.java:42-48` [design] Defaults `max*Count = 0` mean a `Capabilities` that forgot to call `inputs(...)` rejects every data set with an unhelpful "does not allow more than 0 input variables". Default to `Integer.MAX_VALUE`, or make the builder require both.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/Capabilities.java:194,227` [inconsistency] Missing-target check compares against `df.rowCount()`; missing-input check against `df.rvar(name).size()`. Same quantity, two spellings.
- [test] `tst/rapaio/ml/common` only has `VarSelectorTest` (5 tests, none of which asserts that `auto().withVarNames(...).name()` stays AUTO, so the mode-loss bug is untested). No `CapabilitiesTest`, no `ClassWeightsTest`.

#### Summary (top 5)

1. `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/eval/RegressionEvaluationResult.java:115` — regression CV **test scores are computed on the training fold** (`split.trainDf()`, `trainResult`), so reported test error equals training error; plus only train tables are printed (143-144, 165, 175).
2. `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/eval/split/Split.java:34` — convenience constructor fills **train weights with 0.0** (`VarDouble.fill(n)`), test weights with 1.0; and both evaluators (`RegressionEval.java:172`, `ClassifierEvaluation.java:122`) accept `weights` but never pass them to `fit`.
3. `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/common/distance/MinkowskiDistance.java:39,54,63` — `name()` returns "Euclidean" and `pow(delta, p)` lacks `abs`, giving NaN/cancelling terms; `VarSelector.java:62-75` similarly loses AUTO/ALL mode on `withVarNames`.
4. `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/loss/HuberLossFunction.java:154,168` and `L1LossFunction.java:49` / `HuberLossFunction.java:82` — Huber `alpha` is a quantile in the gradient but a raw threshold in `error()`; weighted minimizers of L1 and Huber ignore weights. No loss tests exist at all.
5. `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ml/eval/ClusterSilhouette.java:206,178` — similarity mode returns silhouettes with the wrong sign; singleton clusters score 1 instead of 0; `compute()` is not re-entrant. Also `RegressionEval.java:126-141` busy-spins a core and swallows fold failures, and `threads` default can be 0.

### 2.7 nn, math, ts, text

# Review: rapaio.nn, rapaio.math, rapaio.ts, rapaio.text

Root: `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib`. Analysis only; no files modified.

#### rapaio.nn (core: Tensor, Autograd, BackFunction, TensorManager)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Tensor.java:208-214` `addGrad` stores the supplied array by reference on first call (`this.grad = grad`). Several back functions return the parent's own grad array or a view of it (`IdentityNode` returns `this.grad`; `AddNode`/`SubNode` return `this.grad().sumTo(shape,false)` which returns `this` when shapes already match, see `BaseDoubleStrideDArray.reduceTo:426-432`; `ReshapeNode`, `StretchNode`, `CatNode` return views). The second `addGrad` on that child then does `add_` in place and silently mutates the parent's (and possibly the user-set root) gradient. Example: `a=x.identity(); b=a.identity(); c=a.mul(3); root=b.add(c)` -> `root.grad` and `b.grad` are corrupted after backward. [bug] Fix: `this.grad = grad.copy()` on first assignment, or make every additive back function return a fresh array (or always use the `Consumer` form that writes into an owned buffer).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Autograd.java:53-59` `backward` requires the caller to have already set `root.grad`; there is no default of ones for a scalar root, unlike every other autograd framework. Users of `Loss` get this for free but any direct `Autograd.backward(x.sum())` throws. [design] Suggest defaulting `grad` to ones when the root is a scalar.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Autograd.java:100` `backFunction.apply();;` double semicolon. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Autograd.java:97-106` `retainGrad` parameter actually controls whether the graph (back functions) is retained, not whether intermediate grads are retained. Name contradicts semantics (PyTorch: `retain_graph`). `resetGrad()` also clears back functions. [inconsistency] Rename to `retainGraph`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Autograd.java:133` topological sort does `frontier.stream().filter(...).findFirst()` in a `while` loop: O(V^2) on the frontier and non-deterministic order because `HashSet` iteration order is hash-based. Use a queue of zero-indegree nodes (Kahn) for O(V+E) and determinism. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Autograd.java:135` "Graph contains cycles" is unreachable: the graph is built by constructors so it is a DAG by construction, but if reached the exception type `IllegalArgumentException` is wrong (state error). [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Autograd.java:150-155` `computeGrad` is built from the leaves up, but the iteration is over `reverse.reversed()`, which is the reversed topological order of the *root-first* list, i.e. leaves first: correct, but the comment/naming is confusing and it relies on `parents` map which was populated from `coverage` (a `HashSet`), so for tensors that appear twice as `ref` of the same parent (e.g. `x.mul(x)`) the parent is added twice; harmless for a `Set` but wasteful. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Autograd.java:82-88` `covered()` exposes the internal mutable list; `printTensors` writes to `System.out` from library code. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Autograd.java` no check that `tensor.requiresGrad()` leaf grads are of the same dtype as the manager; grads are whatever the back function returns. A `Variable` created with a DOUBLE array in a FLOAT manager silently mixes dtypes. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/BackFunction.java:40` `protected Tensor ref;` should be `private final`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/BackFunction.java:75-80` `UpdatingBackFunction.apply` allocates the grad buffer with `Order.C` but `AdditiveBackFunction` uses whatever the supplier returns; mixing orders is fine functionally but the `Order.C` hard-code is inconsistent with `Order.defaultOrder()` used everywhere else. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Tensor.java:99` `backFunctions` list and `grad`/`value` fields are not thread-safe; `TensorManager.outerExecutor` exists but there is no documented threading model for building graphs in parallel. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Tensor.java:158` `dim(int)` is the only accessor that is not `final`, unlike `shape()`, `rank()`, `size()`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Tensor.java:286-291` `split(axis, indices...)` produces `narrow(axis, indices[i], next)` but never yields the segment `[0, indices[0])`; callers must pass `0` first, undocumented. Also `indices.length == 0` returns an empty list instead of the whole tensor. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Tensor.java:354-384` `std1d`/`stdOn` hard-code `epsilon = 1e-3` as a default; this is unusually large (PyTorch/BatchNorm default 1e-5) and undocumented. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Tensor.java:434-436` `log()` passes `eps = -1` as the NaN replacement for the gradient (`LogNode`), which means `d/dx log(x)` at `x=0` becomes `-1` instead of `+inf`/large positive. A negative gradient replacement flips descent direction. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Tensor.java` no `mm`/`matmul`, `t()`, `sum` with keepDim, `mean()` over all; `bvtm` is the only linear-algebra op. Missing ops force users into `Linear`. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/TensorManager.java:68` `new Random()` (java.util.Random) is not thread-safe and is shared across the outer executor threads via `random()`. Use `SplittableRandom`/`ThreadLocalRandom` or document single-threaded use. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/TensorManager.java:45-55` `ofFloat`/`ofDouble` duplicate the thread-count computation; no factory that takes explicit thread counts although the constructor supports them. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/TensorManager.java:114` `System.err.println` in library code. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/TensorManager.java:127-178` factory methods named `var`, `scalarTensor`, `zerosTensor` ... while array variants are `zerosArray`; the `var()` vs `*Tensor()` naming is inconsistent. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/TensorManager.java:184-194` three overloads of `zerosArray` where the `(DType, Shape)` one ignores the manager dtype: this is used by `UpdatingBackFunction` only; leaks the dtype knob for no reason. [nit]

#### rapaio.nn.tensors (autograd nodes)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/IdentityNode.java:32` returns `this::grad` directly (aliasing, see core finding #1). Additionally the forward does `child.value().copy()`, while backward shares the grad: asymmetric. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/shape/ReshapeNode.java:33` backward `this.grad.reshape(x.shape())` uses default order; if the forward was asked with `askOrder=F` and the grad is C-ordered the element mapping of reshape differs (reshape is order-dependent). Should reshape with the same order used in forward, or use `reshape(shape, askOrder)` on a `reorder(askOrder)` of the grad. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/shape/NarrowNode.java:33-36` uses the `Consumer` (in-place) back function form, whereas `CatNode`, `StretchNode`, etc. use the `Supplier` form; the two coexist without a rule. `NarrowNode` is the only correct one for the aliasing issue. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/shape/CatNode.java:49` returns a `narrow(...)` view of `this.grad`; with aliasing in `addGrad` a child that receives it first and then accumulates another grad will write into the cat node's grad. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/shape/CatNode.java:33-46` no validation that all inputs share the same `TensorManager`/dtype or rank; `arrays` is a `List<? extends DArray<?>>` passed to `cat`. Also `tensors.length == 0` would throw an obscure error. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/StretchNode.java:32` `this.grad.squeeze(axis)` is a view of parent's grad (aliasing). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/GatherNode.java:34-35` `zeros` is allocated once at construction and reused by `scatter` each time the back function is applied; with `retainGrad=true` and two backward passes, the second scatter accumulates into the same buffer that was already handed to `x.addGrad` (double counting). Allocate inside the lambda. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/BatchVtmNode.java:33-36` backward for `bv` computes `grad.bvtm(bm.t())` and, if `bv.rank()==1`, uses `mean1d(0)` over the batch. The correct reduction for a broadcast input is `sum`, not `mean`: `y_b = v^T M_b` for all `b`, so `dL/dv = sum_b M_b g_b`. Gradient is off by a factor `1/batch`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/BatchVtmNode.java:37` backward for `bm` is `bv^T · grad` as a single `mm`, which only matches the `(b,n) x (n,m)` (shared matrix) case; for the `(b,n) x (b,n,m)` batched-matrix case the weight grad should be per-batch outer products `(b,n,m)`, and for `(n) x (n,m)` the `t()` of a rank-1 array is questionable. No shape dispatch on the four documented forms. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/BatchVtmNode.java:32` forward forces `reorder(Order.C)`/`reorder(Order.F)` copies on every call; this is a performance choice hidden in a node, undocumented. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/LogSoftmaxNode.java:32-36` formula `g - softmax * sum(g)` is correct. Uses `this.value().exp()` each backward instead of caching. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/SoftmaxNode.java:32-37` `s*g - s*sum(s*g)` correct. `p.sub_(ws)` in-place on a fresh temp: fine. [ok]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Std1dNode.java:31-37` `dof = dim - ddof` is used in the backward (`div_(dof)`) but the value is `sqrt(var1d(ddof) + eps)`: correct only if `var1d` divides by `dim-ddof`. However the back edge to `mu` returns zeros: the derivative wrt an *externally supplied* mean is not zero (`d std/d mu = -(x-mu).sum/(dof*std)`, which is zero only when `mu` is the actual sample mean). When a user passes a different `mean` tensor the gradient is wrong; when `mu` is internal it is a pointless edge that also forces the mean node into the graph. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Std1dNode.java:33` `centered` is computed with `mu.value().stretch(axis)` but `var1d(axis, ddof, mu.value())` receives the un-stretched mean; inconsistent contract between the two calls (works only because `var1d` stretches internally). [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/StdOnNode.java:32,37` `dof = x.size() - ddof` is computed but never used; backward divides by `shape.size()` ignoring `ddof`, so for `ddof=1` the gradient does not match the value (`var` divides by `n-ddof`). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/StdOnNode.java:34-35` `centered = x - mu` with `mu` of reduced shape relies on broadcast; but `meanOn(shape)` without `keepDim=true` (`x.meanOn(shape)` -> `MeanOnNode` uses keepDim=true, ok) whereas an external `mean` may not be keepDim. Undocumented requirement. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/StdOnNode.java:38` `tm.zerosTensor(mu.shape()).value()` creates a `Variable` just to get an array; `Std1dNode` uses `tm.zerosArray`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Standardize1dNode.java:43-52` backward for standardization `t1 - (sum(g) + vs*sum(g*vs))/N / std` is the textbook formula for `ddof=0`; with `ddof != 0` the `vs*sum(g*vs)` term should be scaled by `N/(N-ddof)`. Since the constructor accepts `ddof` this is a mismatch. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/StandardizeOnNode.java:34` node is named `Standardize1dNode.class.getSimpleName()` (copy-paste). [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/StandardizeOnNode.java:50` same `ddof` mismatch as `Standardize1dNode`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Standardize*Node.java:55-61` `outputMean()`/`outputStd()` wrap internal arrays in fresh `Variable`s each call, detached from the graph, but hold no `requiresGrad`; naming suggests outputs of the node. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/DropoutNode.java:38-45` samples `Bernoulli.of(p)` and *keeps* the element when the sample is 0: keep probability `1-p`, scaled by `1/(1-p)`: correct inverted dropout. But `p==1` divides by zero (`div_(0)` -> NaN/inf) and `p<0 || p>1` is not validated. Also the mask is built with a per-element `Bernoulli.sampleNext` through a `PointerIterator`: slow versus a vectorised `random` array. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/DropoutNode.java:46-47` `inplace=true` mutates `child.value()` in place; any *other* node built from `child` before this one (e.g. a residual branch) now sees dropped values in its cached forward value, and the child's own back functions (e.g. `SigmoidNode` uses `this.value()`) compute wrong gradients. Nothing prevents or documents this. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/DropoutNode.java:51` backward `grad*mask` forgets the `1/(1-p)` scale applied in forward; gradient is `(1-p)` times too small. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/DropoutNode.java:37` mask is a BYTE array; `mul(mask)` mixes dtypes and relies on implicit promotion. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/CompareTrueNode.java:35` / `CompareFalseNode.java:35` two near-identical classes differing by `neg_().add_(1)`; could be one node with a `negate` flag. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/binary/AddNode.java:38-39` / `SubNode`, `MulNode`, `DivNode`: `sumTo(shape, false)` correctly reduces broadcast dims; but `sumTo` returns `this` when no reduction is needed (see aliasing bug). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/binary/DivNode.java:39` `grad * (-left / right^2)` correct. Allocates `left.neg()` then `div` then `sqr`: three temporaries where one in-place chain would do. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/binary/SubNode.java:29` is the only binary node that is not `final` and the only one with a descriptive error message; siblings say only "Nodes are not valid for elementwise broadcast." [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/binary/*.java` `left.tm()` is used as manager without checking `right.tm() == left.tm()`. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/unary/LogNode.java:32` builds `ones.div_(x).nanToNum_(eps)`: `1/0 = +inf`, not NaN, so `nanToNum_(eps)` with a single arg may or may not replace infinities (depends on `nanToNum_(double)` semantics). If it does, `eps=-1` (the default from `Tensor.log()`) gives a negative gradient at zero. Also allocating a full ones tensor via `tm.fullTensor(...)` (a `Variable`) to get an array. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/unary/SqrtNode.java:31-32` forward `sqrt().nanToNum_(0)` maps negative inputs to 0; backward `grad/(2*value)` divides by zero for `x<=0` producing inf/NaN, with no matching `nanToNum_`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/unary/SigmoidNode.java:32` `s*(1-s)` correct; `TanhNode` `1-t^2` correct; `ExpNode`, `NegNode`, `SqrNode` correct. [ok]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/reduce/MaxNode.java:33` `max(threshold)` is really a ReLU-with-threshold; the name collides with a reduction (`reduce` package) and with what users expect of `max`. Gradient uses `GT` so at `x == threshold` gradient is 0: fine. [inconsistency] Rename to `clampMin`/`threshold`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/reduce/MeanOnNode.java:31` sets `this.value = ...` directly instead of `setValue`, unlike every other node; and backward returns `this.grad().div(shape.size())` relying on broadcast back to `x.shape()` inside `addGrad`'s `add_` — but on *first* `addGrad` the reduced-shape array is stored as `x.grad` directly (wrong shape until a second accumulation broadcasts). [bug] Expand explicitly to `x.shape()`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/reduce/SumNode.java:33` `tm.scalarArray(x.value().sum().doubleValue())` round-trips through `double` even for FLOAT: fine, but backward loops `strexp` per rank instead of `expand` to shape. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/reduce/Sum1dNode.java:32` / `Mean1dNode.java:35` return `strexp` views (stride 0). If this becomes `x.grad` on first `addGrad` and later something `add_`s into it, the stride-0 view writes the same memory `dim` times. [bug] (same root cause as aliasing.)
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/reduce/Mean1dNode.java:31` validates rank; `Sum1dNode` does not. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Variable.java:30-37` a `Variable` with `null` value is constructible and `shape()` will NPE. [design]

#### rapaio.nn.tensors (convolutions)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv2dNode.java:54-57` `outputPadding = max(outputPaddingH, outputPaddingW)` and a single value is passed to `convTranspose2d`. When H and W need different output paddings (non-square inputs with stride>1) the input gradient has the wrong spatial shape or is misaligned. [bug] Fix: pass separate H/W output paddings (API change on `convTranspose2d`) or pad the result explicitly.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv1dNode.java:57-77` weight gradient loops over batch and group, allocating an `unfold1d` per sample; `mm(other, to)` accumulates so the loop is correct, but it is O(batch) Java-level iterations of small matmuls. A single unfold over the batch and one `mm` would be faster. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv1dNode.java:72` / `Conv2dNode.java:81` `gradW.narrow(...).reshape(...)` is assumed to be a *view* so that `mm(..., to)` writes into `gradW`. If `reshape` on a narrowed (non-contiguous) array falls back to a copy, the weight gradient is silently zero for groups > 1. Nothing asserts contiguity. [bug] Add a check or build per-group grads and `cat`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv1dNode.java:42` / `Conv2dNode.java:42` no validation of `stride>0`, `dilation>0`, `padding>=0`, `groups` dividing channels; errors surface from deep inside `DArray`. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv1dNode.java:82` bias grad `sum1d(2).sum1d(0)` correct; `Conv2dNode.java:90` `sum1d(3).sum1d(2).sum1d(0)` correct. [ok]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv1dNode.java:48-51` computes `lIn` from `x.value().dim(2)` at construction but the back functions read `x.value()` lazily: if `x.value` is replaced (e.g. in-place dropout or `setValue`) shapes and data disagree. Same pattern everywhere (`MulNode` uses `right.value()` lazily). Nodes should capture the forward inputs they need. [design]

#### rapaio.nn.loss

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/loss/NegativeLogLikelihoodLoss.java:50` reduction is inverted: `MEAN ? sum : sum.div(size)`; `MEAN` returns the sum and `SUM` returns a mean. Also the divisor is `pred.value().size()` (batch*classes) whereas `NegativeLikelihoodLoss` divides by `pred.dim(0)` (batch). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/loss/NegativeLogLikelihoodLoss.java:49` `pred.log()` uses `LogNode` with `eps=-1`; for a predicted probability of exactly 0 the gradient is replaced by `-1`. No `log(p+eps)` clamp: `-log(0)=inf` loss. No numerically stable log-softmax + NLL path (the usual "cross entropy" loss). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/loss/NegativeLikelihoodLoss.java` vs `NegativeLogLikelihoodLoss.java` names are swapped relative to convention: `NegativeLikelihoodLoss` takes log-probabilities (`-gather(pred)`), i.e. it *is* NLL in PyTorch terms, while `NegativeLogLikelihoodLoss` takes probabilities and applies `log`. Neither Javadoc says which input is expected. [inconsistency] Rename to `NLLLoss` (log-probs) and `CrossEntropyLoss` (logits, computed via logsoftmax).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/loss/MSELoss.java:46-48` `if (pred.value().isMatrix()) y.setValue(y.value().stretch(1))` mutates the *caller's* target tensor and does not check `y` is a vector: calling `forward` twice with the same `y` produces shape `(n,1,1)`, and a matrix `y` gets stretched wrongly. The NLL losses guard with `y.value().isVector()`. [bug] Build a local stretched view instead of `setValue`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/loss/*Loss.java:50-51` every loss calls `last.setGrad(tm.scalarArray(1))` in `forward`, and `AbstractLoss.batchForward` sets it again on the summed tensor; the seed grad belongs in `Autograd.backward` (default ones for scalar root) rather than in each loss. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/loss/AbstractLoss.java:47-60` `batchForward` returns a tensor that is the *sum* of per-batch losses but a `lossValue` that is the *mean* over batches; the reported number and the gradient scale disagree, and the gradient scale grows with the number of batches. Empty `batches` -> NPE on `lossTensor.setGrad`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/loss/AbstractLoss.java:52` `this.newInstance()` per batch loses parameters set on the original loss (`reduce`) unless `newInstance` copies params; it does not (`new MSELoss(tm)`). A `reduce(SUM)` loss silently becomes `MEAN` inside `batchForward`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/loss/AbstractLoss.java:35` `protected String name` is never assigned or read. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/loss/AbstractLoss.java:54` `trueValues.value().sel(0, batch.indices())` re-slices the whole target for each batch; fine but the `Batch` already carries indices—the dataset could carry targets. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Loss.java:47-52` `Output.backward()` has no `retainGraph` overload, so a user cannot backprop the same loss twice. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Loss.java:32` `Loss` requires `tm()` and `newInstance()` but the `reduce` param is not part of the interface, so `batchForward` cannot be written generically. [design]

#### rapaio.nn.optimizer

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/SGD.java:55,68-77,89-91` `mus` is a plain `HashMap` written concurrently from the per-parameter tasks submitted to the thread pool in `step()`. Data race / possible map corruption. Adam uses `ConcurrentHashMap`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/SGD.java:90` first momentum step stores `mu = gt` where `gt` may be the parameter's own `grad` array (when `weightDecay == 0`). The buffer then aliases the gradient; if the user does not call `zeroGrad()` before the next backward, `addGrad` accumulates into the momentum buffer. Clone on first use (PyTorch does `clone().detach()`). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/SGD.java:84` no null check on `tensor.grad()` (NPE with an unhelpful message); Adam throws a descriptive exception. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/SGD.java:70` / `Adam.java:75` a new `Executors.newFixedThreadPool(cpuThreads)` is created and torn down on *every* `step()` call; the `TensorManager.outerExecutor()` exists for exactly this and is unused here. Thread creation per step dominates for small models. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/SGD.java:59` stores the caller's `Collection` by reference (`this.params = params`), while Adam copies into a new list. If the caller's collection is mutated the optimizer changes. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/SGD.java:93-97` update rules: momentum `mu = m*mu + (1-d)*g`, nesterov `g + m*mu`, plain `mu`: match PyTorch. Weight decay added to gradient before momentum: matches PyTorch SGD. [ok]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/SGD.java:44` default `lr = 1e-4` while Adam defaults to `1e-3`; PyTorch's SGD has no default. Arbitrary and undocumented. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/Adam.java:102-116` `m_t`, `v_t`, bias corrections with `t` starting at 1 and incremented after all params: correct. Weight decay is L2-in-gradient (Adam, not AdamW): matches PyTorch `Adam` but the param doc does not say so. [ok]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/Adam.java:111-114` AMSGrad keeps the running max of the *bias-corrected* `avt`; PyTorch keeps the max of the raw `v_t` and then bias-corrects the max. Different (slightly) algorithm; document or align. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/Adam.java:55` `t` is a `double` used only as an integer counter and exponent. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/Adam.java:120-142` three `getX` helpers do `containsKey` then `get` (two lookups, non-atomic); use `computeIfAbsent`. Every step allocates new `mt`/`vt` arrays instead of updating in place (`mul_`, `add_`). [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/Adam.java:83-85` / `SGD.java:78-80` `InterruptedException` is wrapped in `RuntimeException` without re-setting the interrupt flag (TensorManager.close does it right). [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Optimizer.java:58,69` static factory methods named `SGD` and `Adam` (capitalised) violate Java naming and shadow the class names. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/optimizer/NoGrad.java` `zeroGrad()` is a no-op, so a training loop that swaps in `NoGrad` accumulates gradients forever. If it is a null-object it should still zero grads or be documented. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Optimizer.java` no `lr` accessor in the interface (schedulers impossible without casting), no `state()` for checkpointing optimizer moments. [design]

#### rapaio.nn.layer / Network

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:81-82,117-118` the `while (clazz != null)` loops call `this.getClass().getDeclaredFields()` instead of `clazz.getDeclaredFields()`: superclass fields are never scanned and the *same* fields are scanned once per ancestor level. `parameters()` dedups with a `Set` so it only wastes work, but `state()` has no dedup, so every sub-network state is merged 3+ times and `saveState` writes each tensor several times (round-trips only because `loadState` repeats the same mistake). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:86-88` reflection `getMethod("parameters").invoke(...)` on a field already known to be a `Network`; a cast `((Network) field.get(this)).parameters()` is enough. Unchecked cast to `List<Tensor>`. Fields of type `List<Network>`/arrays are not discovered. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:147-168` `batchForward` runs `forward` for all batches concurrently on `outerExecutor`, but `BatchNorm1D.forward11` mutates `sampleMean/sampleStd`, `Dropout` uses the shared `java.util.Random`, and every layer reads the `train` flag: not thread-safe, and running statistics become order-dependent. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:165` `catch (Throwable e) { throw new RuntimeException(e); }` wraps `CompletionException` (already unchecked) and `Error`s. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:241-253` `loadState` reads dtype and shape from the stream then ignores both: no check against the target tensor's shape/dtype, so a mismatched checkpoint corrupts weights silently or throws an index error. Raw `DType` type. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:270-276` `readeByteArray` writes with `ptrSetInt`; method names `readeXxx` are typos. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:55` `train = false` by default: freshly built networks are in eval mode (PyTorch default is train). Dropout/BatchNorm silently behave as inference until `train()` is called; undocumented. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Network.java:43` `Network extends Serializable` but `Tensor`, `TensorManager` (holds an `ExecutorService`) and `DArray` are not serializable; `Serializable` is a false promise. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/Network.java:122-138` default `forward` throws `NotImplementedException` for `xs.length != 1` and `forward11` throws too; a subclass that overrides neither compiles and fails at runtime; make one abstract. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/NetworkState.java:67` exposes the internal mutable `ArrayList`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/Linear.java:58-63` init `U(-1/sqrt(in), 1/sqrt(in))` for both weight and bias matches PyTorch's default. Weight is stored `(in, out)` (transposed vs PyTorch) and used through `bvtm`; fine but `Order.F` forced. [ok]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/Linear.java:35-37` `inFeatures/outFeatures` stored but no validation `> 0`, no getters. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/Conv1D.java:35,65,68` weights `U(-0.5, 0.5)` regardless of fan-in, and bias initialised to **1**. Neither Xavier nor He; a 1-bias shifts every channel. [bug] Use `U(-1/sqrt(fan_in), 1/sqrt(fan_in))` and zero bias.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/Conv2D.java:64,70` range `sqrt(groups / (inChannels*(kH+kW)/2))` uses the *mean* of the kernel sides instead of `kH*kW` (fan_in = inChannels/groups * kH * kW); bias initialised to 1. Inconsistent with Conv1D's constant range. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/Conv1D.java:37-38` / `Conv2D.java:35-36` `inChannels`, `outChannels`, `k`, `kH`, `kW` fields are stored and never read. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/BatchNorm1D.java:57-58,84,93` `sampleMean/sampleStd` are initialised in the constructor so the `== null` branches (including the "requires at least one optimization step" exception) are dead code; eval before any training silently normalises with mean 0 / std 1. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/BatchNorm1D.java:88-89` running statistics are an EMA of the batch *std* (`sqrt(var+eps)`), not of the variance; `EMA(sqrt(v)) != sqrt(EMA(v))`, and the eps is baked into the running std. PyTorch tracks unbiased variance and applies eps at use. Also the training normalisation uses biased variance (`ddof=0`) while running stats should be unbiased. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/BatchNorm1D.java:88-89` each forward replaces `sampleMean`/`sampleStd` with new `Variable` objects, so a `NetworkState` obtained earlier (or an `Optimizer` given `state().tensors()`) points at stale tensors. Update the arrays in place. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/BatchNorm1D.java:82` running stats are updated on *every* train-mode forward, including forwards done only to compute a training-set loss; standard but worth documenting; with `batchForward`'s parallelism the update order is random. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/LayerNorm.java:48-49` `gamma`/`beta` are scalars, not per-element over `shape` (PyTorch `elementwise_affine`). Also eps default `1e-8` (LayerNorm) vs `1e-5` (BatchNorm) vs `1e-3` (`Tensor.std*`): three different epsilons in one package. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/LayerNorm.java:67-71` duplicates the broadcast validation that `StandardizeOnNode`/`DArray.meanOn` will perform anyway. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/Dropout.java:58` passes `tm.random()` (shared, non-thread-safe `Random`) into every dropout node. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/Sequential.java:42-43` `parameters()` does not dedupe shared sub-modules while `AbstractNetwork.parameters()` does; an optimizer would step a shared weight twice. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/Sequential.java:76-79` `forward` returns `null` for an empty sequence instead of the inputs (identity) or throwing. `networks == null` check is impossible. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/Flatten.java:35-44` rank-1 input becomes `(1, n)` (treats a vector as batch of one) while rank-2 passes through; other layers treat dim 0 as batch consistently. Also `Flatten` relies on the reflective default `parameters()/state()` while `ReLU`, `Softmax`, ... override them with empty lists. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/ReLU.java:48` implemented as `x.max(0)` (`MaxNode`): correct; `ELU.java:58-61` `x>0 ? x : alpha*(exp(x)-1)` correct via two masks (`exp(0)-1=0` on the positive side). [ok]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/nn/layer/*.java` `ReLU`, `Sigmoid`, `Tanh`, `Softmax`, `LogSoftmax`, `ELU`, `Dropout` each repeat identical empty `parameters()`/`state()`; an `AbstractStatelessLayer` would remove ~60 lines. [design]

#### rapaio.math.optimization

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/IRLSSolver.java:211,213` `w.div(w.sum()); w.mul(w);` are non-in-place operations whose results are discarded (dead statements). The intent (per the comment structure and the `(p-2)/2` exponent on line 209) was `w.div_(...)` and `w.mul_(w)` so that the weights become `|e|^(p-2)`; as written IRLS0M uses `|e|^((p-2)/2)`, i.e. the wrong weighting. `IRLS1M` line 268 has the same discarded `w.div(w.sum())` (harmless there because it is a global scale). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/IRLSSolver.java:209` for `p == 1`, weights `1/max(tol, |v|)` with `tol = eps = 1e-12` produce weights up to `1e12` and a badly conditioned normal system; `IRLS1M:266` hard-codes `1e-10` instead, inconsistent with `eps`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/IRLSSolver.java:220-225` `catch (RuntimeException ignored)` swallows any failure in the QR solve and reports "not converged"; `IRLS1M:273` has no such guard, so the two methods fail differently on a singular system. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/IRLSSolver.java:263,287` in IRLS1M the residual `e` is computed from the *previous* `x` but appended to `errors` after `x` has been updated, so `errors.get(i)` does not correspond to `solutions.get(i)`; IRLS0M records the error before the update. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/IRLSSolver.java:275` `q = 1/(pk-1)` divides by zero when `pk == 1`; only reached when `p > 2` today, but nothing enforces `p >= 1` on the `p` param (no validator). [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/IRLSSolver.java:84,89,149` the system matrix is the param `m` externally and `A` internally; `b` is `b`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/IRLSSolver.java:48-60` `newMinimizerM1/M0` choose `k` from `p` and then `MethodImpl` clamps it again (`cut(k,1.01,2)` / `cut(k,0.5,0.99)`); two places encode the same rule. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/linesearch/BacktrackLineSearch.java:74-76` Armijo backtracking has no iteration cap and no minimum step: if `p` is not a descent direction (`gxp >= 0`) or `f` is noisy, `t` shrinks until it underflows to 0 (~2000 iterations of `f`) and returns `t = 0`. Also no check that `gxp < 0`. Only the sufficient-decrease condition is tested, never the curvature (Wolfe) condition; the class doc should say "Armijo" rather than imply Wolfe. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/linesearch/BacktrackLineSearch.java:54-55` `alpha` validator `v < 0.5` follows Boyd; fine but undocumented in the param description. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/linesearch/LineSearch.java:45-61` API is `DArray<Double>` only, while `rapaio.nn` is `DArray<?>`; the two halves of the library cannot share functions. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/SteepestDescentSolver.java:105` `abs(error)` on a norm is redundant; `:110` `sol.copy().add(p.mul(t))` copies twice (`add` already allocates). [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/SteepestDescentSolver.java:100,111` stores every iterate (`solutions.add(sol.copy())`) with `maxIt = 100_000` default: O(maxIt * n) memory for a solver. Make trajectory recording optional. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/SteepestDescentSolver.java:101-108` convergence is only `||grad|| < tol` with `tol = 1e-10`; no relative tolerance, no step/function-decrease test, so ill-scaled problems run to `maxIt`. `f`, `d1f`, `x0` default to `null` and are never validated (NPE inside `compute`). [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/SteepestDescentSolver.java:117-119` `toString` NPEs on `sol` before `compute()`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/Solver.java:44` `compute()` returns `Solver`, so `SteepestDescentSolver.newSolver().compute()` loses the concrete type unless overridden (it is), but the interface offers no `lineSearch`/`tol` accessors. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:233` ITP computes `n_half = ceil(ln((b-a)/(2 eps)))` with the natural log; the ITP paper uses `log2`. This inflates `n_max`, enlarges `r`, and loses the method's minmax bound. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:223-233,241-242` after the swap that makes `f(xa) < 0 < f(xb)`, `xa > xb` is possible (decreasing `f`); then `xb - xa < 0`, `log(negative)` is NaN, `(int) ceil(NaN) == 0`, and `pow(xb-xa, k2)` is NaN for non-integer `k2` (allowed by the validator up to ~2.618). The method still terminates through the bracket-width loop but is no longer ITP. Use `Math.abs(xb - xa)` consistently or keep `xa < xb` and track the sign separately. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:138` bisection stops only on `|f(xc)| < eps` (default `1e-15`); with an interval-width criterion missing, functions whose values cannot reach `1e-15` in double precision iterate to `maxIter` (100k) with `converged=false`. Regula falsi, secant, Brent, Ridder use interval width instead. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:58` default `eps = 1e-15` is below the ulp of doubles larger than ~4, so `|xb - xa| < eps` can be unreachable; the tests get away with it only because the roots lie in `[1, 3]`. Use a relative tolerance or `eps ~ 1e-12`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:159-168` regula falsi does not validate finiteness or opposite signs, unlike the other five methods; `171` divides by `fa - fb` without a zero check. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:286-288` secant demands a sign change (`fa*fb > 0` -> throw) although the secant method does not need a bracket; the test at `RootFind1DTest.java:74-79` uses `x0=10, x1=30` for `x^2-612` (root ~24.7) and happens to bracket. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:351` Brent recomputes `f(xc)` every iteration although `fc` is known from the previous step; one extra function evaluation per iteration. `455` Ridder recomputes `f(x5)` for a point whose value is already known. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:140,175,200,293,346` `iterations` is `it+1` in some exits and `it` in others. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:119,159,275` snake_case method names (`bisection_optimize`, `secant_optimization`, ...), a factory called `newModel()` for a root finder, `optimize()` for root finding, params `x0/x1` documented as "a"/"b", and `ridder_optimization` declares a local `x1` shadowing the `x1` param. [nit]

#### rapaio.math.MathTools

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/MathTools.java:1043-1052` `gcd` uses repeated subtraction: `gcd(0, n)` and any negative argument loop forever; `gcd(a, a)` fine. Use Euclid with `%` and `Math.abs`. [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/MathTools.java:1069-1072` `combinations(0, k>0)` returns 1 instead of 0; overflow detection `c < 0` misses wrap-around to a positive value (`Math.multiplyExact` would be correct). [bug]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/MathTools.java:1031` `significantDigits` returns `BigDecimal.scale()` (digits after the decimal point), not the number of significant digits (`precision()`); `significantDigits(100.0) == 1`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/MathTools.java:399-401` `erf(x) = 2*normalCdf(x*sqrt2) - 1` suffers catastrophic cancellation for `|x| << 1` (`erf(1e-10)` loses most digits); `erfc` for large positive `x` is fine but `erf` for large `x` is not. A direct series/continued-fraction `erf` is standard. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/MathTools.java:952,966` `logistic`/`logit` use `StrictMath`; the rest of the class uses `Math`/static-imported `log`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/MathTools.java:1010-1014` `fdist` returns `1 - betaIncReg(...)`, i.e. an upper-tail probability, which is not what "fdist" (CDF) suggests; the loss of precision near 1 is also avoidable via `betaIncReg(d2/(d1 x + d2), d2/2, d1/2)`. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/math/MathTools.java:1002-1007` `eq(a, b)` with absolute `1e-10` tolerance is misleading for large magnitudes; no relative variant. [nit]

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ts/Acf.java:72,79` `var == 0` (constant series) yields `NaN` correlation without a message; a lag `>= ts.size()` silently returns 0. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/ts/Pacf.java:70` `c = a / b` with no guard for `b == 0` (perfectly predictable series) -> `NaN`/`Inf` propagates into all later lags. [bug]

[inconsistency]

#### Tests (tst/rapaio/nn, tst/rapaio/math, tst/rapaio/ts)

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/nn` there is **no numerical (finite-difference) gradient check anywhere**; every backward test compares against hand-computed constants, so systematic formula errors (BatchVtm mean-vs-sum, Dropout scale, Std ddof, Standardize ddof, MeanOn shape) go undetected. Add a generic `gradCheck(Tensor -> Tensor, inputs)` helper using central differences in double precision and run it over every node with random shapes, including broadcast shapes. [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/nn/tensors` no tests for `BatchVtmNode`, `DropoutNode`, `GatherNode`, `StretchNode`, `SoftmaxNode`, `SigmoidNode`, `Std1dNode`, `StdOnNode`, `CompareTrueNode`, `CompareFalseNode`, `SumNode`, `Sum1dNode`, `MeanOnNode`. [test]

[test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/nn/AutogradTest.java` no test that gradient *accumulation* across two paths into the same leaf leaves the upstream grads untouched (would catch the aliasing bug); no test for `retainGrad=false` clearing back functions then a second `backward` failing; no test that a leaf appearing twice in one op (`x.mul(x)`) gets `2x`. [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/nn/AutogradTest.java:47-57` the same 9-node graph is rebuilt verbatim in 5 tests; extract a fixture. Tests reach into package-private `graph.root`, `graph.reverse`, `graph.retainGrad`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv1dNodeTest.java:67-70` / `Conv2dNodeTest.java:94-97` all convolution tests use `stride=1, padding=0, dilation=1, groups=1` and square inputs; the `outputPadding` computation, `groups > 1` weight-gradient path and non-square `Conv2d` are untested. Only `TensorManager.ofDouble()` is exercised. [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/nn/**` `TensorManager` instances are created per test (`@BeforeEach tm = TensorManager.ofFloat()`) and never `close()`d, leaking a fixed thread pool per test. [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1DTest.java:82` `assertTrue(Math.abs(f.apply(x)) <= 1e20)` is vacuous. `:46-53` the method list contains `Bisection` twice and omits `Secant` from the bracketing loop; the loop runs every method on every function (fine) but never tests a decreasing function with non-integer `k2`, nor `maxIter` exhaustion, nor the exception paths. [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/math/optimization/linesearch/BacktrackLineSearchTest.java:71` `assertEquals(0.7, t)` asserts an exact double; fine today but brittle; no test for a non-descent direction (which currently loops ~2000 times). [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/math/optimization` no tests for `SteepestDescentSolver`; `IRLSSolverTest` (2 tests) does not compare IRLS0M against IRLS1M or against a closed-form `p=2` least-squares solution, which would expose the discarded `w.mul(w)`. [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/math/MathToolsTest.java` a single test for a 1000-line utility class; `gcd`, `combinations`, `significantDigits`, `erf` edge cases are untested. [test]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ts/AcfTest.java`, `PacfTest.java` one test each (presumably against R output); no constant-series, short-series or `maxLag >= n` cases. [test]

#### Summary (top 5)

1. **Gradient aliasing in `Tensor.addGrad`** (`Tensor.java:208`): the first gradient handed to a leaf is stored by reference, and many nodes (`IdentityNode`, `AddNode`/`SubNode` via `sumTo` returning `this`, `ReshapeNode`, `StretchNode`, `CatNode`, `Sum1dNode`/`Mean1dNode` stride-0 views) return the parent's own grad or a view of it. Any second accumulation then corrupts upstream (including the user-set root) gradients. Copy on first store or make back functions always return fresh buffers.
2. **Wrong backward formulas**: `DropoutNode` drops the `1/(1-p)` scale; `BatchVtmNode` uses `mean` instead of `sum` for a broadcast vector and a single `mm` for the matrix grad regardless of batching; `Std1dNode`/`StdOnNode`/`Standardize*Node` ignore or misuse `ddof`; `MeanOnNode` returns a reduced-shape grad; `Conv2dNode` collapses H/W output padding into one `max`; `LogNode` default `eps=-1` flips the gradient sign at zero.
3. **Loss/optimizer correctness**: `NegativeLogLikelihoodLoss` inverts MEAN/SUM; `MSELoss.forward` mutates the caller's `y` and stretches it on every call; `AbstractLoss.batchForward` reports a mean while back-propagating a sum and drops `reduce` settings; `SGD` writes a plain `HashMap` from a thread pool and aliases the gradient as its momentum buffer; both optimizers spin up a new thread pool per step.
4. **Layer/network state**: `AbstractNetwork.parameters()/state()` scan `this.getClass()` instead of `clazz` (superclass params missed, state duplicated per ancestor level); `BatchNorm1D` replaces its running-stat tensors every forward, tracks EMA of std instead of variance, and has dead `null` checks; `Conv1D/Conv2D` use ad-hoc weight ranges and initialise bias to 1; `batchForward` runs stateful layers concurrently.
5. **math**: `IRLSSolver` discards `w.div(...)`/`w.mul(w)` results (wrong IRLS0M weights); `BacktrackLineSearch` has no iteration cap and can spin ~2000 times on a non-descent direction; `RootFind1D` ITP uses `ln` instead of `log2` and breaks for decreasing brackets, bisection lacks a width criterion and `eps=1e-15` is below double ulp for `|x|>4`; `MathTools.gcd` loops forever on 0 or negative input. Test coverage: no finite-difference gradient checks, no layer/loss/optimizer tests, no `rapaio.text` tests.

### 2.8 util, io, printer, sys, datasets, build

# Review: rapaio-lib util / io / printer / sys / datasets / module-info / pom

Root: `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib`. Analysis only, no files modified.

#### module-info.java / pom.xml

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/module-info.java` — packages present in `src` but not exported: `rapaio.darray.manager.base`, `rapaio.darray.storage.wrapper`, `rapaio.data.string`, `rapaio.ml.model.knn`. If any public type there is returned by an exported API, callers cannot name it. [inconsistency] Fix: export them or make them package-private/internal by design and document.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/module-info.java:7` — `requires java.sql;` but no source file in `src` references `java.sql`. Dead module dependency that drags `java.sql` (and `java.xml`, `java.logging`) into every consumer's module graph. [inconsistency] Fix: remove.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/module-info.java:118` — `exports rapaio.util.parralel;` propagates the package-name typo (`parralel`) into the public module API; renaming later is a breaking change, so do it now. [inconsistency]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/pom.xml:86-99` and `/home/ati/work/rapaio/pom.xml:71-84` — `maven-compiler-plugin` configuration (version, `--enable-preview`, `--add-modules`) is duplicated verbatim in parent and child. Child also repeats `maven-surefire-plugin` with a different `argLine`. [inconsistency] Fix: keep it once in the parent (`pluginManagement`), override only `argLine` in the child.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/pom.xml:117` — surefire `argLine` starts with `${argLine}` which is only defined when the jacoco `prepare-agent` goal runs. Running `mvn surefire:test` or a profile that skips jacoco leaves the literal string `${argLine}` on the JVM command line and the fork fails. [bug] Fix: define `<argLine></argLine>` as an empty default property in the pom.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/pom.xml:117` — `-javaagent:${org.mockito:mockito-core:jar}` depends on `maven-dependency-plugin:properties` (line 101) executing first; the plugin has no `<version>` and no `<phase>`, so it runs at the default `initialize` phase only by luck of the default lifecycle binding. [nit] Fix: pin version and phase explicitly.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/pom.xml:150-162` — `maven-javadoc-plugin` config declares `<additionalOptions>` twice (`-Xdoclint:none` at 152 and `--enable-preview` at 156). Maven takes the last one, so `-Xdoclint:none` is silently dropped and only `additionalJOption` still carries it. Also `<show>private</show>` publishes private members in the released javadoc jar. [bug] Fix: single `<additionalOptions>` with both values (or `<additionalOptions><additionalOption>..</additionalOption>...`), `show=protected`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/pom.xml:93,152,156` — `--enable-preview` is passed to compiler, surefire, and javadoc but **no source under the reviewed scope uses a preview feature** (grep for preview APIs found nothing). Shipping a jar compiled with `--enable-preview` forces every consumer JVM to run with `--enable-preview` too (class file minor version 0xFFFF). [bug] Fix: drop `--enable-preview` everywhere unless a preview feature is really used; if it is, document the consumer requirement.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/pom.xml:130-146` — jacoco `report` is bound to `test` phase; with `reuseForks=true` and parallel modules fine, but the `destFile`/`dataFile` under `coverage-reports/` differs from the default the parent's surefire expects (no `${argLine}` in parent). Child and parent are out of sync. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/pom.xml:33` — `<scm>` has only `<url>`; `maven-release-plugin` (configured in child, line 167) needs `<connection>`/`<developerConnection>` to tag. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/pom.xml:86-95` — `maven-deploy-plugin` `skip=true` at the parent while `central-publishing-maven-plugin` `autoPublish=true`/`waitUntil=published` is unconditional: any `mvn deploy` (even a CI dry run) will publish to Maven Central. [design] Fix: move central-publishing to a `release` profile (the child already names one at `releaseProfiles`).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/pom.xml:69-73` — test scope dependency on `io.github.padreati:rapaio-datasets:8.2.0` with a hardcoded version rather than `${project.version}`; will break on every version bump. [nit]

#### util.collection — Ints

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:249-250` — `equals(a, aStart, b, bStart, len)` loops `for (i = 0; i < len - aStart; i++)`. With `aStart > 0` it compares fewer than `len` elements (and with `aStart >= len` compares nothing and returns `true`). `Doubles.equals` (line 484) is correct; this one is not. [bug] Fix: `i < len`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:1570-1577` — `parallelQuickSort(int[] x, int[] y, from, to)` is missing the `else`: for small ranges it sorts sequentially **and then** still creates a `ForkJoinPool` and sorts again. Same bug in `Doubles.java:1673`. [bug] Fix: add `else`, as in the single-array overloads.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:769,1010,1253,1570` — every `parallelQuickSort*` builds a fresh `ForkJoinPool(availableProcessors())` per call; `pool.shutdown()` inside try-with-resources is redundant with `close()`. Creates N threads per sort call. [design] Fix: use `ForkJoinPool.commonPool()` or a shared static pool.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:88-94` — `seq(start, end)` with `end < start` throws `NegativeArraySizeException` rather than IAE or returning empty. Same for `from(source,start,end,fun)` and `Doubles.newSeq`, `Floats.newSeq`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:285-288` — `isDenseArray` reads `indexes[0]` before checking length; empty input throws AIOOBE. [bug] Fix: return `true`/`false` explicitly for empty.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:312-315` — `argmax(array, start, end)` guards `array.length == 0` but not `start >= end`, so an empty *range* on a non-empty array returns `start` (reads `array[start]` even if `start == array.length` → AIOOBE). [bug] Fix: `if (start >= end) return -1`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:338-339` — `EMPTY_ARRAY` and `DEFAULT_EMPTY_ARRAY` are two identical constants; only `EMPTY_ARRAY` is used. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java` (`quickSort` at 3 overloads) — small ranges use `selectionSort` while `insertionSort` (both comparator and natural variants) is defined and only used by `mergeSort`. fastutil, which this code is derived from, uses `selectionSort` intentionally, but the dead `insertionSort(comp)` variants add ~40 lines. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:1156,1281` — `quickSortIndirect(perm, x)` / `parallelQuickSortIndirect(perm, x)` sort `0..x.length` but the array being permuted is `perm`; when `perm.length < x.length` this is an AIOOBE, when longer it silently ignores the tail. [bug] Fix: use `perm.length` (fastutil does) and `ensureSameLength`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java` `stabilize` — calls `parallelQuickSort` per run of equal keys (spawning pools for runs ≥ 8192 only, sequential otherwise). Fine, but the method is O(n) pool-creations worst case; see pool comment above. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:264-279` — `removeIndexesFromDenseSequence` boxes into `Set<Integer>` for a dense int range; a `boolean[]` of size `end-start` is simpler and 10x faster. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:277` — `containsDuplicates` boxes as well; use `IntOpenHashSet` from the same package. [nit]

#### util.collection — IntOpenHashSet / Int2IntOpenHashMap

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/IntOpenHashSet.java:47,~120` — `MISSING = Integer.MIN_VALUE` is the empty-slot sentinel, and `add(Integer.MIN_VALUE)` returns `false` without storing (`array[hash] == value` is hit first on an empty slot); `contains(MIN_VALUE)` returns `false`. The set silently cannot hold `Integer.MIN_VALUE` and no exception is thrown (the map at least throws IAE for `MISSING` keys). [bug] Fix: throw IAE, or track a `containsMin` flag.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/IntOpenHashSet.java:185-191` — `needsCapacity(increment)` and `ensureCapacity(increment)` ignore `increment`; `addAll` therefore may rehash many times. With `allocation ∈ {0,1}`: `floor(0.75*len) == 0`, so `needsCapacity` is true, `ensureCapacity` computes `len = ceil(2*0/0.75) = 0`, and `% 0` throws `ArithmeticException`. [bug] Fix: `len = max(DEFAULT_ALLOCATION, ceil((size+increment)/loadFactor)*2)`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/IntOpenHashSet.java` `Probing.QUADRATIC` (and `Int2IntOpenHashMap`) — probing adds `round*round` cumulatively (`hash += step*step; hash %= len`) on a table whose size is **not** a power of two or prime (16, then `ceil(2*size/0.75)`). Quadratic probing only visits all slots under specific size/step conditions; on the wrong size the probe sequence cycles over a subset and `add`/`put` can spin forever when all slots in that cycle are taken (load factor 0.75 makes this rare but possible). Also `step*step` overflows `int` after 46341 steps → negative index → AIOOBE. [bug] Fix: power-of-two table with triangular probing (`hash += ++step`), or bound the probe count and rehash.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/IntOpenHashSet.java:42` — `DEFAULT_SEEED` typo in a public constant (used from `Int2IntOpenHashMap.keySet`). [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/IntOpenHashSet.java:167` — `int i = (int) o;` unused local; the preceding `instanceof` loop iterates the collection twice. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/IntOpenHashSet.java` — no `remove`, no `equals/hashCode`, implements `Serializable` but not `Set`; `iterator()` copies the whole table (`toArray()`) each call. [design]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Int2IntOpenHashMap.java:200-207` — `values()` filters on `array[i+1] != MISSING` (the *value*) instead of the key slot. A legitimately stored value `Integer.MIN_VALUE` is dropped from `values()`. [bug] Fix: test `array[i] != MISSING`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Int2IntOpenHashMap.java:~100` — `get` returns `MISSING` for absent keys, but `MIN_VALUE` is also a valid value → ambiguous. [design] Document, or add `getOrDefault`.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Int2IntOpenHashMap.java:188-192` — `keySet()` sizes the set with `ceil(size * loadFactor)` (should be `size / loadFactor`): the returned set immediately rehashes while being filled. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Int2IntOpenHashMap.java:31-52` vs `IntOpenHashSet.java:~200` — identical `Probing` enum copy-pasted into both classes; two incompatible types. [inconsistency] Fix: one top-level enum.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/collection/Int2IntOpenHashMap.java` — `DEFAULT_ALLOCATION = 12` vs set `16`; interleaved key/value layout doubles `array.length`, so `allocation` means "entries" here but "slots" in the set. [inconsistency]
- No `remove`, `containsValue`, `equals/hashCode`; `Serializable` without a version-safe format (fields include a `Probing` enum reference — fine, but the seed is not validated on read). [design]

#### util.hash — Murmur3

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/hash/Murmur3.java:40-48` — `murmur3A(int)` serializes the int **big-endian** (`value>>24` first) and then the block loop reads **little-endian**, so `murmur3A(int)` ≠ reference `MurmurHash3_x86_32` of the int's native LE bytes (e.g. Guava `murmur3_32().hashInt(v)`), and `murmur3A(double)` uses `ByteBuffer` default big-endian. Results are internally consistent but not interoperable with the reference. [inconsistency] Fix: write LE bytes (`(byte) value, (byte)(value>>8), ...`) or document.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/hash/Murmur3.java:40-48` — allocates a `byte[]` per call; hash-set/map hot paths call this per probe. [design] Fix: inline the single-block mix for the 4-byte case (no allocation).
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/hash/Murmur3.java` `murmur3A(byte[], offset, len, seed)` — no bounds validation; `len < 0` yields `roundedEnd < offset` and then `switch(len & 3)` reads before `offset`. [nit]
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/hash/Murmur3.java` `murmur3F` — reference `MurmurHash3_x64_128` seeds `h1 = h2 = seed` as unsigned 32-bit; done correctly. Tail `case 8: k1 = ((long) key[roundedEnd+7]) << 56` is fine (top byte). Verified against Yonik's reference: matches. No issue.
- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/hash/Murmur3.java` — no `murmur3A(long)`/`murmur3A(String)` convenience matching the `int/double` ones; `CharSequence` variant requires `offset/len/seed`. [nit]

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/Pair.java:59-61` — `update(Pair<T1,T2> p)` calls `update(v1, v2)` with **its own** fields, ignoring `p`: a no-op. [bug] Fix: `update(p.v1, p.v2)`.

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/Hardware.java:46-112` — a `static` initializer spawns external processes (`wmic`, `sysctl -a hw`, `cat /proc/cpuinfo`) at **class-load time**; it is triggered from `DArrayManager` and all `Base*StrideDArray`, so every first use of darray forks a process. `wmic` is removed on Windows 11 24H2+, so on modern Windows this always logs a warning. - `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/Hardware.java:88-101` — LINUX branch reads `cache size` from `/proc/cpuinfo`, which on x86 is the **L3** (last-level) size, not L2; constant is named `L2_CACHE_SIZE` and used for blocking in darray. - `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/Hardware.java:114-131` — `readCmdLines` never drains stderr, never calls `waitFor()` or `destroy()`; a chatty stderr can block the child and the child's handles leak. - `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/util/parralel/ParallelStreamCollector.java:257-266` — any exception in one mapped element `handle()`s → `shortCircuited=true` and `dispatcher.shutdownNow()`; the exception surfaces only via `join()` as `CompletionException`, and the remaining futures are cancelled. - `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/io/atom/BinaryAtomProtocol.java:189-193` — `readBytes` ignores the return value of `readNBytes(buffer, 0, len)`: a truncated stream yields a silently zero-padded array instead of `EOFException`. [bug] Fix: `inputStream.readFully(buffer)`.

- `/home/ati/work/rapaio/rapaio-lib/src/rapaio/io/atom/AtomRegistry.java:33-40` — lazy singleton with unsynchronized `if (INSTANCE == null) INSTANCE = new ...`; two threads can create two registries and `register()` calls made on one are lost.
